<a id='question-118'></a>

### Question 118: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-18 (– Involvement of mandible or maxilla is characteris)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the recognition of characteristic clinical features and histological findings of specific leukemias, particularly those involving extramedullary sites like the jaw, and the ability to differentiate them based on morphology and immunophenotype. (本题考察特定白血病，特别是累及颌骨等髓外部位的白血病的特征性临床表现和组织学发现，以及根据形态学和免疫表型进行鉴别的能力。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male presents to his dentist complaining of persistent, localized pain and swelling in his lower right jaw, which has been worsening over the past 3 months. He denies fever, night sweats, weight loss, or recent infections. He has no significant past medical history and takes no medications. Intraoral examination reveals a firm, non-tender, erythematous mass involving the gingiva and alveolar bone of the right mandibular premolar region. Radiographs show a lytic lesion destroying the mandibular bone. A biopsy of the gingival mass is performed. Histopathology reveals sheets of medium-sized mononuclear cells with round nuclei, finely dispersed chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and abundant, pale blue cytoplasm. Mitotic figures are present. Immunohistochemical staining shows the cells are positive for CD19, CD10, and BCL6, and negative for CD5, CD23, and TdT. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a t(8;14) translocation.

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) with gingival hyperplasia
(B) Burkitt Lymphoma
(C) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL)
(D) Follicular Lymphoma
(E) Mantle Cell Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old male. Age is relevant as certain lymphomas have age predilections.
*   **Presenting Complaint:** Persistent localized pain and swelling in the lower jaw (mandible) for 3 months. Jaw involvement is a key feature for certain hematologic malignancies.
*   **Systemic Symptoms:** Absence of B symptoms (fever, night sweats, weight loss) is noted, which can be present but not always in lymphomas.
*   **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender, erythematous mass involving gingiva and alveolar bone. Lytic bone lesion on radiographs. This points towards an infiltrative process affecting bone.
*   **Histopathology:** Sheets of medium-sized mononuclear cells, round nuclei, finely dispersed chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, abundant pale blue cytoplasm, mitotic figures. This morphology is characteristic of high-grade lymphoma cells.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD19 (B-cell marker), CD10 (germinal center marker), BCL6 (germinal center marker), negative for CD5 (usually negative in follicular lymphoma, positive in CLL/SLL and MCL), CD23 (usually negative in follicular lymphoma, positive in CLL/SLL), TdT (terminal deoxynucleotidyl transferase, marker of immature lymphocytes, usually negative in mature B-cell lymphomas).
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14) translocation. This is the hallmark translocation for Burkitt lymphoma, involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14.

These clues collectively point towards a high-grade B-cell lymphoma with germinal center features, frequently involving extranodal sites like the jaw, and characterized by the t(8;14) translocation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Burkitt Lymphoma (BL):** A highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. It arises from germinal center B-cells.
*   **Clinical Features:** Often presents as a rapidly growing mass, frequently in extranodal sites, especially in the jaw (endemic form), abdomen (sporadic form), or chest (immunodeficiency-associated form). Jaw involvement is particularly characteristic of the endemic form seen in equatorial Africa, but can occur sporadically.
*   **Morphology:** Characterized by monotonous population of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely dispersed chromatin, multiple nucleoli (though sometimes inconspicuous), and abundant basophilic cytoplasm containing lipid vacuoles. High mitotic rate and apoptosis ("starry sky" appearance due to tingible body macrophages).
*   **Immunophenotype:** B-cell lineage (CD19+, CD20+, CD22+). Expresses germinal center markers like CD10 and BCL6. Typically negative for CD5, CD23, and TdT (although TdT can be weakly positive in some cases).
*   **Genetics:** Defined by translocations involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8, most commonly t(8;14)(q24;q32) in ~80% of cases, leading to MYC overexpression. Less common translocations involve MYC and immunoglobulin light chain loci, t(2;8) or t(8;22). MYC is a transcription factor crucial for cell proliferation, growth, and apoptosis. Its dysregulation drives the rapid proliferation characteristic of BL.
*   **Pathogenesis:** MYC overexpression leads to uncontrolled cell cycle progression and inhibition of differentiation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) with gingival hyperplasia:** AML is a malignancy of myeloid precursors. While gingival hyperplasia can occur in AML (especially monocytic subtypes, M4/M5), the morphology described (medium-sized mononuclear cells with round nuclei, dispersed chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli) is not typical for AML blasts, which usually have more irregular nuclei, coarser chromatin, and prominent nucleoli. The immunophenotype (CD19+, CD10+, BCL6+) is characteristic of B-cells, not myeloid cells. AML does not typically involve the t(8;14) translocation.
- (C) **Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL):** DLBCL is the most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. It is a heterogeneous group of aggressive B-cell lymphomas. While it can involve the jaw and express CD19, CD20, and sometimes CD10/BCL6, it typically lacks the specific t(8;14) translocation and the characteristic morphology of Burkitt lymphoma (often more pleomorphic cells). DLBCL cells are usually larger than Burkitt lymphoma cells.
- (D) **Follicular Lymphoma:** Follicular lymphoma is an indolent (low-grade) B-cell lymphoma arising from germinal center B-cells. It typically presents with widespread lymphadenopathy. While it expresses CD19, CD10, and BCL6, it is characteristically positive for CD23 and negative for BCL6 in a significant proportion of cases (though BCL6 positivity can occur). It is associated with the t(14;18) translocation involving BCL2, not t(8;14). The morphology consists of small cleaved cells and centrocytes, not the monotonous medium-sized cells described. Jaw involvement is less common than in Burkitt lymphoma.
- (E) **Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** Mantle cell lymphoma is an aggressive B-cell lymphoma arising from naive B-cells in the mantle zone of lymph follicles. It typically expresses CD19, CD20, CD5, and cyclin D1. It is negative for CD10 and CD23. The characteristic translocation is t(11;14) involving the CCND1 gene (encoding cyclin D1). The morphology usually consists of small to medium-sized lymphocytes with irregular nuclei. Jaw involvement is uncommon.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (BL) | DLBCL                 | Follicular Lymphoma | Mantle Cell Lymphoma | AML (Gingival Hyperplasia) |
| :------------------ | :-------------------- | :-------------------- | :------------------ | :------------------- | :-------------------------- |
| **Cell Type**       | B-cell (Germinal Ctr) | B-cell (Germinal Ctr/Post-GC) | B-cell (Germinal Ctr) | B-cell (Mantle Zone) | Myeloid Precursor           |
| **Morphology**      | Medium, round nuclei, fine chromatin, inconspicuous nuc, basophilic cytoplasm, high mitotic rate | Large, variable morphology | Small cleaved/centrocytes | Small to medium, irregular nuclei | Blasts (variable morphology) |
| **CD19**            | +                     | +                     | +                   | +                    | -                          |
| **CD10**            | +                     | +/-                   | +                   | -                    | -                          |
| **BCL6**            | +                     | +/-                   | +                   | -                    | -                          |
| **CD5**             | -                     | +/-                   | -                   | +                    | -                          |
| **CD23**            | -                     | +/-                   | +                   | -                    | -                          |
| **TdT**             | -/+                   | -                     | -                   | -                    | +                          |
| **Translocation**   | t(8;14)                | Variable              | t(14;18)            | t(11;14)             | Variable                   |
| **Jaw Involvement** | Common                | Possible              | Uncommon            | Uncommon             | Possible (M4/M5)            |
| **Clinical Course** | Very Aggressive       | Aggressive            | Indolent            | Aggressive           | Acute                       |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by MYC translocation (most commonly t(8;14)), aggressive clinical course, and frequent involvement of extranodal sites, particularly the jaw. Differentiating it from other lymphomas requires attention to morphology, immunophenotype (CD19+, CD10+, BCL6+, CD5-, CD23-), and specific genetic translocations. Key differentials include DLBCL (more variable morphology, lacks t(8;14)), Follicular Lymphoma (indolent, t(14;18), CD23+), and Mantle Cell Lymphoma (CD5+, t(11;14)). AML can cause gingival hyperplasia but has different morphology, immunophenotype, and genetics.

<a id='question-119'></a>

### Question 119: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-19 (Epstein Barr virus)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection is strongly associated with specific types of lymphomas, particularly in immunocompromised individuals, and can lead to characteristic cellular changes and clinical presentations. (EBV 感染与特定类型的淋巴瘤密切相关，尤其是在免疫功能低下者中，并可导致特征性的细胞变化和临床表现。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male patient with a history of HIV infection (CD4 count 180 cells/µL) presents with rapidly progressive fatigue, night sweats, and a palpable mass in the right side of his neck. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. A biopsy of the cervical lymph node shows a diffuse proliferation of atypical lymphoid cells with prominent nucleoli and abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm. These cells are positive for CD20, CD30, and PAX5, and negative for CD3 and CD15. Immunohistochemical staining reveals strong positivity for latent membrane protein 1 (LMP1). Flow cytometry shows a monoclonal B-cell population. The patient's serum is positive for EBV viral capsid antigen (VCA) IgM and IgG antibodies. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Burkitt lymphoma
(B) Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), germinal center B-cell like, associated with HIV
(C) Hodgkin lymphoma, classical
(D) Primary effusion lymphoma (PEL)
(E) Follicular lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 55-year-old male with HIV (CD4 count 180 cells/µL) - Indicates significant immunosuppression, increasing risk for opportunistic infections and specific malignancies like EBV-associated lymphomas.
*   **Symptoms:** Rapidly progressive fatigue, night sweats, palpable neck mass, generalized lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly - Classic "B symptoms" and signs of widespread lymphoid involvement, suggestive of lymphoma.
*   **Lymph Node Biopsy:** Diffuse proliferation of atypical lymphoid cells, prominent nucleoli, abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm - Morphological features consistent with a high-grade lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+, CD30+, PAX5+, CD3-, CD15- - Indicates a B-cell lineage (CD20+, PAX5+), excludes T-cell lymphoma (CD3-). CD30 positivity is seen in some B-cell lymphomas, including DLBCL and Hodgkin lymphoma. CD15 negativity argues against classical Hodgkin lymphoma.
*   **LMP1 Positivity:** Strong positivity for Latent Membrane Protein 1 (LMP1) - A hallmark of EBV infection in B-cells, strongly associated with certain lymphomas, particularly in immunocompromised hosts.
*   **Flow Cytometry:** Monoclonal B-cell population - Confirms the neoplastic nature of the B-cells.
*   **EBV Serology:** Positive VCA IgM and IgG - Indicates current or recent EBV infection/reactivation.
*   **Synthesis:** The combination of HIV-associated immunosuppression, rapid onset, B-cell lymphoma morphology, CD20+/CD30+/PAX5+ immunophenotype, LMP1 expression, and EBV seropositivity points strongly towards an EBV-associated B-cell lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **EBV and Lymphomagenesis:** EBV is an oncogenic virus, particularly implicated in certain lymphomas, especially in immunocompromised individuals (e.g., HIV, post-transplant). It infects B-lymphocytes.
*   **Latent Infection:** EBV establishes latent infection in B-cells, expressing specific latent proteins like LMP1, LMP2A, EBNA1, EBNA2, and EBNA3.
*   **LMP1 Function:** LMP1 acts as a constitutively active oncogene, mimicking CD40 signaling. It activates NF-κB, promoting cell survival, proliferation, and inhibiting apoptosis. This contributes significantly to B-cell transformation.
*   **HIV and EBV:** HIV infection impairs T-cell control over EBV-infected B-cells, leading to increased EBV load and a higher risk of developing EBV-associated lymphomas, such as DLBCL (often germinal center B-cell like) and Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **DLBCL, Germinal Center B-cell like (GCB):** This subtype of DLBCL is characterized by morphology resembling germinal center B-cells, expression of BCL6, and often associated with MYC rearrangements or BCL2/BCL6 fusions. It is frequently associated with EBV, particularly in HIV patients. The immunophenotype (CD20+, CD30+, PAX5+) is consistent with this subtype.
*   **Burkitt Lymphoma:** Also strongly associated with EBV (especially endemic type) and MYC translocations (t(8;14)). While possible in HIV, the CD30+ phenotype is less typical for Burkitt lymphoma (usually CD10+, BCL6+, CD30-).
*   **Hodgkin Lymphoma:** Characterized by Reed-Sternberg cells (CD30+, CD15+). CD15 negativity makes classical Hodgkin lymphoma less likely. EBV is associated with a subset of Hodgkin lymphoma, but the overall immunophenotype here doesn't fit.
*   **Primary Effusion Lymphoma (PEL):** A rare B-cell lymphoma associated with HHV-8 (Kaposi sarcoma-associated herpesvirus), not EBV. Typically presents with pleural or pericardial effusions.
*   **Follicular Lymphoma:** Usually a low-grade lymphoma with a characteristic follicular growth pattern and t(14;18) translocation involving BCL2. Not typically associated with EBV or CD30 positivity.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Burkitt lymphoma:** While strongly associated with EBV and occurring in HIV patients, Burkitt lymphoma typically has a different immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+, CD30-) and morphology (starry sky pattern). The CD30+ result in the vignette makes this less likely.
- (C) **Hodgkin lymphoma, classical:** Classical Hodgkin lymphoma is characterized by Reed-Sternberg cells that are typically CD30+ and CD15+. The biopsy description doesn't mention Reed-Sternberg cells, and the cells are CD15-. While EBV is associated with some cases, the immunophenotype and morphology are inconsistent.
- (D) **Primary effusion lymphoma (PEL):** PEL is associated with HHV-8, not EBV. It typically presents with effusions and has a distinct immunophenotype (CD79a+, CD10-, CD30-, CD45-). This diagnosis is inconsistent with the EBV association and immunophenotype provided.
- (E) **Follicular lymphoma:** Follicular lymphoma is typically a low-grade lymphoma with a follicular pattern, associated with the t(14;18) translocation and BCL2 overexpression. It is not typically associated with EBV or CD30 positivity, and the morphology described (diffuse proliferation of atypical cells) is not characteristic.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | DLBCL (GCB-like, EBV+) | Burkitt Lymphoma | Hodgkin Lymphoma (cHL) | PEL                  | Follicular Lymphoma |
| :------------------ | :-------------------- | :--------------- | :--------------------- | :------------------- | :------------------ |
| **EBV Association** | Strong (esp. HIV)     | Strong           | Moderate               | None (HHV-8)         | Weak                |
| **HIV Association** | Strong                | Moderate         | Moderate               | Moderate             | Weak                |
| **Morphology**      | Diffuse, atypical     | Starry sky       | Reed-Sternberg cells   | Sheets of atypical cells | Follicular pattern  |
| **Immunophenotype** | CD20+, CD30+, PAX5+    | CD10+, BCL6+, CD30- | CD30+, CD15+           | CD79a+, CD10-, CD30- | CD10+, BCL2+, CD20+ |
| **LMP1**            | Often Positive        | Variable         | Variable               | Negative             | Negative            |
| **Key Translocation**| Variable (MYC, BCL2/6) | t(8;14) MYC      | Rare                   | None                 | t(14;18) BCL2        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
EBV is a key oncogenic virus associated with specific lymphomas, particularly in immunocompromised hosts like HIV patients. Recognizing the characteristic immunophenotype (CD20+, CD30+, PAX5+, LMP1+) in this clinical context strongly suggests an EBV-associated B-cell lymphoma, most commonly DLBCL, germinal center B-cell like subtype. Differentiating this from other EBV-associated lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma relies on careful attention to morphology and the full immunophenotype (e.g., CD10, BCL6, CD30). Remember that Hodgkin lymphoma has CD15+, PEL is associated with HHV-8, and Follicular lymphoma has a distinct morphology and genetic hallmark.

<a id='question-120'></a>

### Question 120: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-20 (• American type: nonendemic,sporadic form)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of Burkitt lymphoma based on clinical presentation, morphology, and immunophenotype, differentiating it from other high-grade lymphomas and leukemias. (伯基特淋巴瘤的鉴别诊断，基于临床表现、形态学和免疫表型)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old male from the Midwestern United States presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, intermittent fever, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss (10 lbs). He denies any prior history of immunosuppression or known malignancy. Physical examination reveals a large, palpable mass in the lower abdomen, extending to the midline, and generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and inguinal regions. His spleen is also palpable 5 cm below the left costal margin. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 1200 U/L (normal < 250 U/L), hyperuricemia (uric acid 9.5 mg/dL, normal < 7.0 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9.5 g/dL, normal 13.5-17.5 g/dL). A CT scan of the abdomen and pelvis confirms a large intra-abdominal mass involving the small bowel and retroperitoneal lymph nodes. An excisional biopsy of a cervical lymph node is performed. Histopathology reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are arranged in sheets with interspersed macrophages, some containing phagocytosed cellular debris ("starry sky" appearance). Immunohistochemistry shows the tumor cells positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, but negative for BCL2 and CD30. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation t(8;14)(q24;q32). Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL)
(B) Follicular Lymphoma
(C) Burkitt Lymphoma
(D) Mantle Cell Lymphoma
(E) Hodgkin Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** Young adult male (28 years old) from a non-endemic region (Midwestern US). This points away from endemic Burkitt lymphoma (Africa) and towards the sporadic (American) type.
*   **Symptoms:** Rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, fever, night sweats, weight loss. These are classic "B symptoms" associated with aggressive lymphomas. The rapid growth and abdominal location are highly suggestive of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Physical Exam:** Large abdominal mass, generalized lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly. Consistent with widespread lymphoma involvement.
*   **Labs:** Elevated LDH (tumor marker for high cell turnover/lysis), hyperuricemia (due to rapid cell turnover and lysis releasing purines), anemia (bone marrow involvement or chronic disease). These findings indicate a high-grade, rapidly proliferating malignancy.
*   **Imaging:** CT confirms large intra-abdominal mass involving bowel and retroperitoneal nodes. Common sites for Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, "starry sky" appearance (macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic tumor cells). This morphology is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ (B-cell marker), CD10+ (germinal center marker), BCL6+ (germinal center marker), MUM1+ (plasma cell differentiation marker, often seen in Burkitt), BCL2- (important negative marker, distinguishes from Follicular Lymphoma), CD30- (distinguishes from Hodgkin Lymphoma and some DLBCL subtypes). This immunophenotype is highly specific for Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) involving the MYC gene (on chromosome 8) and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus (on chromosome 14). This translocation is the defining genetic hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma, leading to MYC overexpression and uncontrolled cell proliferation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma characterized by a specific chromosomal translocation, most commonly t(8;14)(q24;q32). This translocation places the MYC proto-oncogene (located at 8q24) under the control of strong immunoglobulin heavy chain enhancers (located at 14q32). This leads to constitutive overexpression of MYC, a transcription factor that drives cell proliferation, metabolism, and apoptosis inhibition. The resulting cells proliferate rapidly, leading to the clinical presentation of a fast-growing tumor with high rates of cell turnover and lysis, explaining the elevated LDH and uric acid levels. The "starry sky" appearance is due to numerous apoptotic tumor cells being phagocytosed by macrophages. The immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-) is characteristic of germinal center B-cells undergoing malignant transformation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL):** DLBCL is the most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. While it can present with similar B symptoms and rapid growth, the morphology is typically more pleomorphic (variable cell size and shape), and the immunophenotype is less specific (CD20+, BCL6+/- CD10+/-). DLBCL lacks the characteristic t(8;14) translocation and the specific immunophenotype (MUM1+, BCL2-). The "starry sky" pattern is less common.
*   **(B) Follicular Lymphoma:** Follicular lymphoma is typically an indolent (slow-growing) lymphoma, often presenting with widespread lymphadenopathy but less commonly with rapidly enlarging masses and B symptoms. Histologically, it shows a nodular growth pattern (follicles) rather than diffuse sheets. The immunophenotype is typically CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+ (due to t(14;18) translocation involving BCL2), and MUM1-. The t(8;14) translocation is absent.
*   **(D) Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** Mantle cell lymphoma is characterized by the t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation involving the CCND1 gene (cyclin D1), leading to cyclin D1 overexpression. The typical immunophenotype is CD20+, CD5+, CD10-, CD23-, cyclin D1+. The morphology is often small to medium-sized lymphocytes. The t(8;14) translocation is absent.
*   **(E) Hodgkin Lymphoma:** Hodgkin lymphoma is characterized by the presence of Reed-Sternberg cells (large, multinucleated cells) in a background of reactive inflammatory cells. The immunophenotype of Reed-Sternberg cells is typically CD30+, CD15+, CD20-, CD10-. The t(8;14) translocation is absent. The morphology described in the vignette (diffuse sheets of medium-sized lymphoid cells with "starry sky") is not typical for Hodgkin lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Correct) | DLBCL (Distractor A) | Follicular Lymphoma (Distractor B) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (Distractor D) | Hodgkin Lymphoma (Distractor E) |
| :------------------ | :------------------------- | :------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :------------------------------ |
| **Clinical**        | Rapid growth, B symptoms, abdominal mass | Variable, often B symptoms | Often indolent, widespread nodes | Variable, often nodes/spleen | B symptoms, lymphadenopathy |
| **Morphology**      | Diffuse, medium cells, "starry sky" | Diffuse, pleomorphic | Nodular (follicles) | Variable, often small cells | Reed-Sternberg cells, inflammatory background |
| **Immunophenotype** | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2- | CD20+, BCL6+/- CD10+/- | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+, MUM1- | CD20+, CD5+, CD10-, CD23-, Cyclin D1+ | RS cells: CD30+, CD15+, CD20-, CD10- |
| **Genetics**        | t(8;14)(MYC)                | Variable             | t(14;18)(BCL2)                     | t(11;14)(CCND1)                     | None specific                  |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by rapid proliferation, specific morphology ("starry sky"), immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-), and the t(8;14) translocation involving MYC. Differentiating it from other aggressive lymphomas like DLBCL relies on the specific combination of morphology, immunophenotype, and genetics. Follicular lymphoma is typically indolent and BCL2+. Mantle cell lymphoma is CD5+ and Cyclin D1+. Hodgkin lymphoma has Reed-Sternberg cells and a different immunophenotype.

<a id='question-121'></a>

### Question 121: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-21 (Normal Chromosome)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiation between Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) subtypes based on cytogenetics and morphology, specifically distinguishing AML with normal karyotype (AML-NK) from other AML subtypes like Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL) and AML with t(8;21) or inv(16), emphasizing the role of specific morphological features and molecular markers in diagnosis and prognosis. (区分AML亚型，基于细胞遗传学和形态学，特别是区分核型正常AML (AML-NK) 与其他AML亚型，强调特定形态学特征和分子标记物在诊断和预后中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, fever, and easy bruising. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae, and a 2x3 cm ecchymosis on his left thigh. Laboratory findings show pancytopenia (WBC 2.5 x 10^9/L, Hb 8.5 g/dL, Platelets 30 x 10^9/L). A peripheral blood smear shows numerous blasts with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and Auer rods. Bone marrow aspirate and biopsy confirm the diagnosis of acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Cytogenetic analysis reveals a normal karyotype (46,XY). Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) analysis is negative for PML-RARA, CBFB-MYH11, and RUNX1-RUNX1T1 translocations. Molecular testing reveals a mutation in the *FLT3* gene (internal tandem duplication, ITD). Which of the following is the most likely subtype of AML based on the provided information?

(A) Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL)
(B) AML with t(8;21)(q22;q22.1); RUNX1-RUNX1T1
(C) AML with inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22); CBFB-MYH11
(D) AML with normal karyotype (AML-NK)
(E) AML with myelodysplasia-related changes

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 55-year-old male with fatigue, fever, bruising (symptoms of bone marrow failure).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), ecchymosis (thrombocytopenia).
- **Labs:** Pancytopenia (WBC, Hb, Platelets all low), indicating bone marrow infiltration/failure.
- **Peripheral Smear:** Blasts with Auer rods (characteristic of AML).
- **Bone Marrow:** Confirms AML diagnosis.
- **Cytogenetics:** Normal karyotype (46,XY). This rules out AML subtypes defined by specific chromosomal abnormalities like t(15;17), t(8;21), inv(16), t(16;16), or t(11q23).
- **FISH:** Negative for PML-RARA (APL), CBFB-MYH11 (inv(16)/t(16;16)), and RUNX1-RUNX1T1 (t(8;21)). This further excludes APL and the specific AML subtypes associated with these translocations.
- **Molecular Testing:** *FLT3*-ITD mutation is present. This mutation is common in AML, particularly AML with normal karyotype, and is associated with a poorer prognosis.
- **Question:** Asks for the most likely AML subtype based on the *normal karyotype* and exclusion of specific translocations via FISH.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **AML Classification:** AML is classified based on morphology, cytogenetics, and molecular markers according to the WHO classification.
- **Cytogenetic Subtypes:** Specific chromosomal abnormalities are key diagnostic and prognostic factors in AML.
    - **APL (Auer-Rodlitz syndrome):** Characterized by t(15;17)(q24.1;q21.2) resulting in the PML-RARA fusion gene. Often presents with DIC.
    - **AML with t(8;21):** Characterized by t(8;21)(q22;q22.1) resulting in the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion gene. Generally has a favorable prognosis.
    - **AML with inv(16) or t(16;16):** Characterized by inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22) resulting in the CBFB-MYH11 fusion gene. Generally has a favorable prognosis.
    - **AML with normal karyotype (AML-NK):** Defined by the absence of the specific recurrent genetic abnormalities listed above. This is a heterogeneous group. Molecular mutations like *FLT3*-ITD, *NPM1*, *CEBPA*, *IDH1/2*, *DNMT3A*, *TP53*, etc., are crucial for subclassification and prognosis within AML-NK.
    - **AML with myelodysplasia-related changes:** Occurs in patients with a prior history of myelodysplastic syndrome (MDS) or has specific morphological features (dysplasia) and/or genetic abnormalities (e.g., *TP53*, *ASXL1*, *SF3B1*, *SRSF2*, *U2AF1*, *ZRSR2*, *RUNX1*, *NPM1* without *FLT3*-ITD, *CEBPA* without *TP53* or *IDH1/2* mutations) consistent with MDS.
- **Case Analysis:** The patient has AML confirmed by morphology (blasts with Auer rods) and bone marrow findings. The normal karyotype and negative FISH results for PML-RARA, CBFB-MYH11, and RUNX1-RUNX1T1 specifically exclude APL, AML with t(8;21), and AML with inv(16). The presence of *FLT3*-ITD is common in AML-NK and is associated with a poorer prognosis. Therefore, AML with normal karyotype is the most fitting diagnosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL): Incorrect. APL is defined by the t(15;17) translocation leading to the PML-RARA fusion protein. FISH for PML-RARA would be positive in APL. The vignette explicitly states FISH is negative for PML-RARA. APL also typically presents with DIC, which is not mentioned here, although not always present.
- (B) AML with t(8;21)(q22;q22.1); RUNX1-RUNX1T1: Incorrect. This subtype is defined by the specific t(8;21) translocation. FISH for RUNX1-RUNX1T1 would be positive. The vignette states FISH is negative for RUNX1-RUNX1T1 and the karyotype is normal.
- (C) AML with inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22); CBFB-MYH11: Incorrect. This subtype is defined by the inv(16) or t(16;16) translocation. FISH for CBFB-MYH11 would be positive. The vignette states FISH is negative for CBFB-MYH11 and the karyotype is normal.
- (E) AML with myelodysplasia-related changes: Incorrect. While AML can arise from MDS, this diagnosis requires either a prior history of MDS or specific morphological features of dysplasia in the bone marrow or specific genetic abnormalities associated with MDS (e.g., *TP53*, *ASXL1*, etc.). The vignette describes typical AML morphology (blasts with Auer rods) and cytogenetics (normal karyotype), and the specific mutations associated with MDS-related AML are not mentioned. The presence of *FLT3*-ITD is more characteristic of *de novo* AML, particularly AML-NK.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | APL (t(15;17)) | AML t(8;21) | AML inv(16) | AML-NK (Normal Karyotype) | AML-MDS related |
|----------------------|-----------------|-------------|-------------|---------------------------|-----------------|
| Karyotype           | t(15;17)        | t(8;21)     | inv(16)     | Normal (46,XY)            | Variable (often normal or complex) |
| FISH                | PML-RARA (+)    | RUNX1-RUNX1T1 (+) | CBFB-MYH11 (+) | Negative for specific translocations | Variable (often + MDS markers) |
| Morphology          | Promyelocytes, Auer rods | Blasts, Auer rods | Blasts, Auer rods | Blasts, Auer rods | Dysplastic features often present |
| Common Mutations    | -               | -           | -           | FLT3-ITD, NPM1, CEBPA, etc. | TP53, ASXL1, etc. |
| Prognosis           | Generally good (with ATRA) | Favorable   | Favorable   | Variable (depends on mutation) | Generally poor |
| Clinical Presentation | Often DIC       | -           | -           | Variable                  | Variable |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The WHO classification of AML relies heavily on cytogenetics and molecular markers. A normal karyotype in AML excludes the most common recurrent translocations (t(8;21), inv(16), t(15;17)). The diagnosis then becomes AML with normal karyotype (AML-NK), which is further subclassified by molecular mutations (e.g., *FLT3*-ITD, *NPM1*, *CEBPA*). Differentiating AML subtypes based on cytogenetics and FISH is crucial for prognosis and treatment planning. Key differentials include APL (t(15;17)), AML with t(8;21), and AML with inv(16), which are ruled out by negative FISH and normal karyotype in this case.

<a id='question-122'></a>

### Question 122: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-22 (Involvement of the abdomen (such as bowel, retrope)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Abdominal involvement in leukemia, specifically gastrointestinal (GI) manifestations and retroperitoneal lymphadenopathy, differentiating leukemic infiltration from other causes of abdominal symptoms.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male with a known history of Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL), stage Rai III, presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of worsening abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, and unintentional weight loss of 15 lbs. He reports intermittent episodes of bright red blood per rectum (hematochezia) over the past week. His past medical history is significant for hypertension and hyperlipidemia, managed with lisinopril and atorvastatin, respectively. He denies fever, chills, or night sweats. Physical examination reveals moderate abdominal distension, tenderness to palpation in the right lower quadrant, and hepatosplenomegaly. Vital signs show a temperature of 37.2°C (99.0°F), heart rate of 95 bpm, blood pressure of 130/70 mmHg, and respiratory rate of 18 breaths/min. Laboratory studies show a white blood cell count of 85,000/µL with a predominance of mature lymphocytes, hemoglobin of 9.5 g/dL, and platelets of 120,000/µL. A CT scan of the abdomen and pelvis reveals significant retroperitoneal lymphadenopathy, diffuse bowel wall thickening, and hepatosplenomegaly. An upper endoscopy and colonoscopy are performed. Colonoscopy reveals multiple ulcerated lesions in the cecum and ascending colon, and biopsies are taken. Histopathology of the colonic biopsies shows infiltration of the lamina propria and submucosa by small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. Immunohistochemistry is positive for CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23.

What is the most likely pathological process causing the patient's gastrointestinal symptoms?

(A) Cytomegalovirus (CMV) colitis secondary to immunosuppression from CLL and its treatment.
(B) Non-Hodgkin lymphoma (NHL) involving the gastrointestinal tract, distinct from the patient's known CLL.
(C) Amyloidosis affecting the gastrointestinal tract, secondary to chronic inflammation associated with CLL.
(D) Direct infiltration of the gastrointestinal tract by leukemic cells from the patient's underlying CLL.
(E) Ulcerative colitis, an inflammatory bowel disease, exacerbated by the patient's underlying CLL.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 55-year-old male with known CLL (Rai III). This establishes the underlying hematologic malignancy. Rai III indicates advanced disease with anemia (Hb < 11 g/dL) and/or thrombocytopenia (Plt < 100,000/µL).
*   **Presenting Symptoms:** Abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, weight loss, hematochezia. These suggest significant gastrointestinal (GI) pathology.
*   **Physical Exam:** Abdominal distension, RLQ tenderness, hepatosplenomegaly. Hepatomegaly and splenomegaly are common in CLL due to infiltration. RLQ tenderness and distension point towards potential colonic involvement.
*   **Labs:** High WBC count (85,000/µL) with mature lymphocytes (consistent with CLL), anemia (Hb 9.5 g/dL), thrombocytopenia (Plt 120,000/µL). These confirm active CLL and its complications.
*   **Imaging (CT):** Retroperitoneal lymphadenopathy, diffuse bowel wall thickening, hepatosplenomegaly. Retroperitoneal lymphadenopathy is common in CLL. Bowel wall thickening suggests infiltration or inflammation.
*   **Endoscopy/Colonoscopy:** Ulcerated lesions in cecum/ascending colon. Biopsies taken.
*   **Histopathology:** Infiltration of lamina propria/submucosa by small, mature lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. This morphology is characteristic of CLL cells.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), CD23. This immunophenotype is classic for CLL/SLL (Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma).

The constellation of findings – known CLL, GI symptoms, bowel wall thickening, ulcerated lesions on colonoscopy, and histopathology/immunohistochemistry showing infiltration by CLL cells – strongly points to direct infiltration of the GI tract by the patient's underlying leukemia.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **CLL Pathophysiology:** CLL is a malignancy of mature B lymphocytes. These cells accumulate in the blood, bone marrow, lymph nodes, spleen, and other organs, including the GI tract.
*   **GI Involvement in CLL:** Extranodal involvement, particularly in the GI tract, is a known complication of CLL/SLL. It can manifest as lymphomatous polyposis (multiple small polyps), diffuse infiltration, or focal masses. Symptoms include abdominal pain, bleeding, obstruction, perforation, and malabsorption.
*   **Histopathology:** The infiltrating lymphocytes in the GI tract have the characteristic morphology and immunophenotype of CLL cells (small, mature lymphocytes, CD5+, CD19+, CD20dim, CD23+).
*   **Mechanism:** The leukemic cells circulate and home to various tissues, including the GI tract, where they infiltrate the lamina propria, submucosa, and muscularis propria, leading to inflammation, ulceration, and functional impairment.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **CMV colitis:** While patients with CLL are immunosuppressed and at risk for opportunistic infections like CMV, the histopathology described (small, mature lymphocytes with CLL immunophenotype) is not typical for CMV colitis, which shows characteristic intranuclear and intracytoplasmic inclusions and inflammation. The immunophenotype strongly argues against this.
- (B): **NHL involving the GI tract:** Although NHL can involve the GI tract, the patient has a known diagnosis of CLL. The morphology and immunophenotype of the infiltrating cells are consistent with CLL, not a separate NHL. While CLL/SLL is technically a type of NHL, the question asks for the cause of the GI symptoms *in this patient*, which is the known CLL.
- (C): **Amyloidosis:** Amyloidosis can occur in CLL (AL amyloidosis) and can affect the GI tract, causing symptoms like malabsorption, bleeding, or obstruction. However, the histopathology would show amyloid deposits (appearing amorphous, eosinophilic material on H&E stain, staining positive with Congo red), not lymphocytic infiltration. The described biopsy findings rule this out.
- (E): **Ulcerative colitis:** Ulcerative colitis is an inflammatory bowel disease characterized by chronic inflammation limited to the colon, typically starting in the rectum and extending proximally. Histopathology shows crypt abscesses, architectural distortion, and inflammation involving the mucosa and submucosa. The described biopsy findings (lymphocytic infiltration with CLL phenotype) are inconsistent with ulcerative colitis.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature                 | CLL GI Infiltration (Correct Answer) | CMV Colitis (Distractor A) | NHL (Distractor B) | Amyloidosis (Distractor C) | Ulcerative Colitis (Distractor E) |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :------------------------ | :----------------- | :-------------------------- | :-------------------------------- |
| **Underlying Condition** | Known CLL                            | Immunosuppression (CLL)   | De novo NHL       | Chronic Inflammation (CLL)  | Inflammatory Bowel Disease       |
| **Histopathology**      | Small, mature lymphocytes (CLL cells) | Viral inclusions, inflammation | Lymphoma cells    | Amyloid deposits           | Crypt abscesses, mucosal inflammation |
| **Immunophenotype**     | CD5+, CD19+, CD20dim, CD23+          | N/A                      | Variable (NHL)     | N/A                        | N/A                               |
| **Typical Symptoms**    | Abdominal pain, bleeding, obstruction | Diarrhea, abdominal pain, bleeding | Abdominal pain, bleeding, obstruction | Malabsorption, bleeding, obstruction | Diarrhea, abdominal pain, bleeding |
| **Key Finding**         | CLL cells in GI biopsy               | CMV inclusions            | Lymphoma cells     | Congo red positive deposits | Crypt abscesses                    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CLL can involve extranodal sites, including the GI tract, causing symptoms like abdominal pain, bleeding, and obstruction. Diagnosis requires biopsy showing infiltration by CLL cells with the characteristic immunophenotype. Differentiating CLL GI involvement from other causes of GI symptoms in CLL patients (like infections or other malignancies) is crucial.

<a id='question-123'></a>

### Question 123: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the recognition of Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV)-associated lymphoproliferative disorders, specifically Primary Effusion Lymphoma (PEL), which is strongly associated with HIV/AIDS and often presents with pleural or pericardial effusions, and its characteristic immunophenotype and morphology. (考察EBV相关淋巴增生性疾病，特别是原发性胸腔淋巴瘤(PEL)，其与艾滋病病毒/艾滋病高度相关，常表现为胸膜或心包积液，以及其特征性免疫表型和形态学。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male with a known history of HIV infection (CD4 count 180 cells/µL) presents to the emergency department with progressive shortness of breath and non-productive cough over the past 2 weeks. He reports recent weight loss and night sweats. Physical examination reveals bilateral pleural effusions and mild lymphadenopathy. Thoracentesis is performed, and the pleural fluid analysis shows a high protein level (4.5 g/dL) and a high LDH level (250 U/L), consistent with an exudative effusion. Cytological examination of the pleural fluid reveals numerous atypical lymphoid cells with large nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and abundant basophilic cytoplasm. These cells are positive for CD20, CD79a, and HHV-8 (Human Herpesvirus 8), and negative for CD10 and BCL6. Flow cytometry confirms a monoclonal B-cell population. A biopsy of an enlarged lymph node shows a diffuse proliferation of large atypical lymphoid cells with a "flower cell" morphology, positive for CD20, CD79a, HHV-8, and negative for CD10 and BCL6.

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Burkitt Lymphoma
(B) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL), ABC subtype
(C) Primary Effusion Lymphoma (PEL)
(D) Follicular Lymphoma
(E) Mantle Cell Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old male with HIV (CD4 count 180 cells/µL) - This immediately points towards opportunistic infections or HIV-associated malignancies. Low CD4 count signifies advanced immunosuppression.
*   **Presenting Symptoms:** Progressive shortness of breath, non-productive cough, weight loss, night sweats - Suggestive of a systemic illness, potentially involving the pleura or lungs, and constitutional symptoms common in lymphomas.
*   **Physical Exam:** Bilateral pleural effusions, mild lymphadenopathy - Pleural effusions are a key finding, especially in the context of HIV. Lymphadenopathy is common in lymphomas.
*   **Pleural Fluid Analysis:** Exudative effusion (high protein, high LDH) - Indicates inflammation or malignancy within the pleural space.
*   **Cytology:** Atypical lymphoid cells (large nuclei, prominent nucleoli, abundant basophilic cytoplasm) - Suggests a lymphoproliferative disorder involving the pleura.
*   **Immunophenotype (Pleural Fluid & Lymph Node Biopsy):** CD20+, CD79a+, HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6- - This specific immunophenotype is crucial. CD20 and CD79a confirm B-cell lineage. HHV-8 positivity is strongly associated with certain lymphomas, particularly those seen in immunocompromised individuals. CD10- and BCL6- help differentiate from follicular lymphoma and germinal center-derived DLBCL.
*   **Morphology (Lymph Node Biopsy):** Diffuse proliferation, large atypical cells, "flower cell" morphology - "Flower cells" are characteristic of PEL, although not always present.
*   **Synthesis:** The combination of HIV/AIDS, pleural effusions containing atypical B-cells, and the specific immunophenotype (CD20+, CD79a+, HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6-) strongly points towards Primary Effusion Lymphoma (PEL). PEL is a subtype of Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL) specifically associated with HHV-8 infection, most commonly seen in immunocompromised patients, particularly those with HIV. It often presents as effusions (pleural, pericardial, peritoneal) rather than solid masses.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Primary Effusion Lymphoma (PEL):** A subtype of DLBCL characterized by neoplastic B-cells accumulating primarily in body cavities (pleural, pericardial, peritoneal) rather than forming solid masses.
*   **Etiology:** Strongly associated with Human Herpesvirus 8 (HHV-8), also known as Kaposi's sarcoma-associated herpesvirus (KSHV). HHV-8 infection is nearly universal in PEL cases.
*   **Immunosuppression:** Occurs predominantly in immunocompromised individuals, especially those with HIV/AIDS.
*   **Pathogenesis:** HHV-8 encodes viral proteins (e.g., v-cyclin, v-Myc) that dysregulate cell cycle control and promote B-cell proliferation.
*   **Immunophenotype:** Typically positive for B-cell markers (CD20, CD79a), HHV-8 (LNA-1), and negative for CD10 and BCL6. Often positive for MUM1/IRF4.
*   **Morphology:** Cells are usually large with abundant basophilic cytoplasm, prominent nucleoli, and sometimes a "flower cell" appearance (nuclear grooves).
*   **Clinical Presentation:** Often presents with effusions, ascites, or pleural thickening. Lymphadenopathy is less common than in other DLBCL subtypes.
*   **Treatment:** Similar to other DLBCL subtypes, typically involving chemotherapy regimens like R-CHOP (Rituximab, Cyclophosphamide, Doxorubicin, Vincristine, Prednisone).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Burkitt Lymphoma:** This is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma typically associated with MYC translocations (t(8;14), t(2;8), t(8;22)). It often presents as a rapidly growing mass, commonly in the jaw (endemic form) or abdomen (sporadic form). While it can occur in HIV patients, the immunophenotype is usually CD10+, BCL6+, and HHV-8 negative. The presentation with prominent effusions and the specific immunophenotype (HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6-) make Burkitt lymphoma unlikely.
- (B) **Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL), ABC subtype:** DLBCL is the most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. While PEL is technically a subtype of DLBCL, the ABC subtype is defined by specific genetic alterations (mutations in genes encoding components of the B-cell receptor signaling pathway, such as MYD88, CD79B, CD79A, BCL2, PIK3CA) and lacks immunoglobulin heavy chain gene rearrangements. The clinical presentation (predominant effusions) and the strong association with HHV-8 and specific immunophenotype (HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6-) are more characteristic of PEL than typical ABC subtype DLBCL, which usually presents as nodal or extranodal masses.
- (D) **Follicular Lymphoma:** This is a low-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by a follicular growth pattern and the t(14;18) translocation involving the BCL2 gene. It is typically CD10+ and BCL6+. The immunophenotype (CD10-, BCL6-) and aggressive clinical presentation (effusions, systemic symptoms) are inconsistent with follicular lymphoma.
- (E) **Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** This is a B-cell lymphoma characterized by the t(11;14) translocation involving the CCND1 gene (cyclin D1). It typically expresses CD5 and cyclin D1. The immunophenotype (HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6-) and lack of CD5 expression make mantle cell lymphoma unlikely.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Primary Effusion Lymphoma (PEL) | Burkitt Lymphoma | DLBCL (ABC) | Follicular Lymphoma | Mantle Cell Lymphoma |
| :------------------ | :------------------------------ | :--------------- | :---------- | :------------------ | :------------------- |
| **Association**     | HIV/AIDS, HHV-8                 | MYC translocation | Variable   | t(14;18)            | t(11;14)             |
| **Presentation**    | Effusions (pleural, pericardial) | Masses           | Masses     | Nodal/Extranodal    | Nodal/Extranodal     |
| **Immunophenotype** | CD20+, CD79a+, HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6- | CD10+, BCL6+     | Variable   | CD10+, BCL6+        | CD5+, Cyclin D1+     |
| **Morphology**      | Large cells, "flower cells"      | "Starry sky"     | Variable   | Follicular pattern  | Variable             |
| **HHV-8**           | Positive                        | Negative         | Variable   | Negative            | Negative             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
PEL is a specific subtype of DLBCL strongly associated with HHV-8 and HIV/AIDS, often presenting with effusions and a characteristic immunophenotype (CD20+, CD79a+, HHV-8+, CD10-, BCL6-). Differentiating PEL from other aggressive B-cell lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma (CD10+, BCL6+, HHV-8-) and DLBCL (variable immunophenotype) requires careful attention to clinical context, morphology, and immunophenotyping, especially HHV-8 status.

<a id='question-124'></a>

### Question 124: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-24 (Both endemic and sporadic forms of Burkitt lymphoma)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of Burkitt lymphoma, specifically its characteristic morphology, genetic translocation, and clinical presentation, differentiating it from other high-grade lymphomas and leukemias. (本题考察伯基特淋巴瘤的理解，包括其特征性形态学、基因易位和临床表现，并将其与其他高级别淋巴瘤和白血病区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 12-year-old boy from a rural region of equatorial Africa presents with a rapidly enlarging mass in his jaw, causing facial swelling and difficulty eating. He also reports intermittent fevers and night sweats over the past month. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass involving the mandible, along with generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the neck and axillae. Laboratory tests show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1500 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are interspersed with numerous tangible body macrophages (TBMs) phagocytosing apoptotic debris. Immunohistochemistry is positive for CD20, CD10, and BCL6, and negative for CD5 and BCL2. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8.

Which of the following is the most likely genetic translocation associated with this patient's condition?

(A) t(11;14)(q13;q32)
(B) t(8;14)(q24;q32)
(C) t(14;18)(q32;q21)
(D) t(9;22)(q34;q11)
(E) t(15;17)(q24;q21)

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 12-year-old boy from equatorial Africa. This demographic and geographic location are highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection and *Plasmodium falciparum* malaria.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, generalized lymphadenopathy. This constellation of symptoms points towards a high-grade lymphoma. The jaw involvement is particularly characteristic of endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia. High LDH is common in rapidly proliferating tumors, especially lymphomas and leukemias, due to high cell turnover and necrosis. Hyperuricemia results from the massive cell lysis releasing purines, which are metabolized to uric acid. This is often referred to as "tumor lysis syndrome."
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, and numerous tangible body macrophages (TBMs). This "starry sky" appearance (due to TBMs amidst the dark blue nuclei of the lymphoma cells) is a classic morphological feature of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD20 (B-cell marker), CD10 (germinal center marker), and BCL6 (germinal center marker), negative for CD5 (typically negative in Burkitt, unlike CLL/SLL) and BCL2 (Burkitt lymphoma characteristically lacks BCL2 expression, unlike follicular lymphoma). This immunophenotype is consistent with a germinal center B-cell origin, characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Cytogenetics:** Translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8. This is the defining genetic abnormality in Burkitt lymphoma.

The question asks for the most likely genetic translocation. Based on the clinical presentation, histopathology, immunohistochemistry, and the known association with the MYC gene, the diagnosis is Burkitt lymphoma. The characteristic translocation in Burkitt lymphoma involves the MYC oncogene on chromosome 8 and an immunoglobulin gene locus, most commonly immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) on chromosome 14.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Its pathogenesis is driven by the dysregulation of the MYC proto-oncogene.
- **MYC Translocation:** The MYC gene encodes a transcription factor crucial for cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. In Burkitt lymphoma, MYC is juxtaposed with highly active immunoglobulin gene enhancers (either IGH on chromosome 14, IGK on chromosome 2, or IGL on chromosome 22) due to chromosomal translocations.
- **Mechanism:** This juxtaposition leads to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein, driving uncontrolled cell proliferation and inhibiting differentiation. The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32), involving the MYC gene on 8q24 and the IGH locus on 14q32. Less common variants include t(2;8)(p12;q24) and t(8;22)(q24;q11).
- **Clinical Correlation:** The rapid proliferation driven by MYC overexpression explains the aggressive clinical course, high LDH levels (due to high cell turnover and necrosis), and hyperuricemia (tumor lysis syndrome). The endemic form is associated with EBV, which likely contributes to B-cell immortalization and proliferation, setting the stage for MYC dysregulation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) t(11;14)(q13;q32): This translocation involves the CCND1 gene (encoding cyclin D1) on chromosome 11 and the IGH locus on chromosome 14. It is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma. The clinical presentation and morphology described (starry sky, CD10+) are not typical for Mantle Cell Lymphoma, which usually presents with more indolent disease, involves lymph nodes and spleen, and has a different immunophenotype (CD5+, cyclin D1+).
- (C) t(14;18)(q32;q21): This translocation involves the IGH locus on chromosome 14 and the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18. It is the hallmark of Follicular Lymphoma. Follicular lymphoma typically presents as indolent, nodular disease, and the lymphoma cells express BCL2 protein, which is explicitly negative in the described case.
- (D) t(9;22)(q34;q11): This translocation, known as the Philadelphia chromosome, involves the ABL1 gene on chromosome 9 and the BCR gene on chromosome 22, creating the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene. It is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and a subset of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). The clinical presentation (jaw mass, starry sky morphology, B-cell immunophenotype) is inconsistent with CML or Philadelphia chromosome-positive ALL.
- (E) t(15;17)(q24;q21): This translocation involves the PML gene on chromosome 15 and the RARA gene on chromosome 17, creating the PML-RARA fusion gene. It is the defining genetic abnormality of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). APL presents with pancytopenia, coagulopathy (DIC), and characteristic promyelocytes with Auer rods in the bone marrow and peripheral blood. The clinical presentation and morphology described are completely different from APL.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14)) | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | CML/Ph+ ALL (t(9;22)) | APL (t(15;17)) |
|----------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------|---------------------------------|-----------------------|-----------------|
| **Typical Age**      | Children/Young Adults     | Middle-aged/Elderly             | Middle-aged/Elderly             | Adults                | Adults          |
| **Clinical**         | Jaw mass, rapid growth    | Lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly   | Nodular lymphadenopathy         | Fatigue, splenomegaly | Pancytopenia, DIC |
| **Morphology**       | Starry sky, medium cells  | Small lymphocytes, mantle zones  | Follicular pattern, centroblasts | Myeloid hyperplasia   | Promyelocytes, Auer rods |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2- | CD5+, CD19+, Cyclin D1+         | CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+             | CD34+, Myeloid markers | CD34+, Myeloid markers |
| **Genetic**          | t(8;14) (MYC-IGH)         | t(11;14) (CCND1-IGH)            | t(14;18) (IGH-BCL2)             | t(9;22) (BCR-ABL1)    | t(15;17) (PML-RARA) |
| **LDH/Uric Acid**    | High                      | Variable                        | Variable                        | Variable              | Variable        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by rapid proliferation, a "starry sky" morphology, and a specific genetic translocation involving the MYC gene (most commonly t(8;14)). Differentiating it from other lymphomas requires knowledge of their characteristic genetic translocations, immunophenotypes, and clinical presentations. Key differentials include Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14), CD5+), Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18), BCL2+), CML/Ph+ ALL (t(9;22)), and APL (t(15;17)).

<a id='question-125'></a>

### Question 125: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-25 (children and young adults.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the ability to differentiate between acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL) and acute myeloid leukemia (AML) based on clinical presentation, peripheral blood smear findings, and bone marrow biopsy results, specifically focusing on cytogenetic abnormalities and their prognostic significance in pediatric leukemia. (考察儿童白血病，特别是ALL和AML的鉴别诊断，包括临床表现、外周血涂片、骨髓活检结果，以及细胞遗传学异常及其预后意义。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 7-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, pallor, and easy bruising. His mother reports recent frequent nosebleeds and a low-grade fever. Physical examination reveals petechiae on his lower extremities, hepatosplenomegaly, and cervical lymphadenopathy. Complete blood count (CBC) shows pancytopenia (WBC 2.5 x 10^9/L with 80% blasts, Hb 7.5 g/dL, Platelets 20 x 10^9/L). A peripheral blood smear shows medium-sized cells with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm, some with Auer rods. Bone marrow aspiration and biopsy confirm hypercellularity with 90% blasts. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow reveals a translocation t(15;17)(q24;q21).

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis and associated prognostic factor?

(A) Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) with t(8;21)(q22;q22); favorable prognosis.
(B) Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL) with t(12;21)(p13;q22); favorable prognosis.
(C) Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) with t(15;17)(q24;q21); poor prognosis.
(D) Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL) with t(9;22)(q34;q11); poor prognosis.
(E) Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) with inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22); favorable prognosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** A 7-year-old boy presents with symptoms suggestive of bone marrow failure (fatigue, pallor, bruising, nosebleeds) and systemic involvement (fever, hepatosplenomegaly, lymphadenopathy). This points towards a hematologic malignancy affecting the bone marrow.
- **CBC Results:** Pancytopenia (low WBC, Hb, Platelets) with a high percentage of blasts (80% in peripheral blood, 90% in bone marrow) confirms acute leukemia.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Medium-sized cells with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm are characteristic features of lymphoblasts, suggesting Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). However, the presence of Auer rods is a specific marker for myeloid lineage, strongly suggesting Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). This discrepancy is a key point.
- **Bone Marrow Findings:** Hypercellularity with 90% blasts confirms acute leukemia.
- **Cytogenetic Analysis:** The presence of the translocation t(15;17)(q24;q21) is pathognomonic for Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most likely diagnosis and associated prognostic factor.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Diagnosis:** The clinical presentation (pancytopenia, blasts), bone marrow findings (hypercellularity, >20% blasts), and specific cytogenetic abnormality t(15;17) definitively point to Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML (M3 according to the older FAB classification). The presence of Auer rods in the peripheral smear, although typically associated with AML, can occasionally be seen in ALL, but the t(15;17) translocation is specific for APL.
- **Mechanism of t(15;17):** This translocation fuses the PML gene on chromosome 15 with the RARA (retinoic acid receptor alpha) gene on chromosome 17, creating the PML-RARA fusion protein. This fusion protein blocks myeloid differentiation at the promyelocyte stage, leading to an accumulation of abnormal promyelocytes in the bone marrow and peripheral blood.
- **Prognosis:** APL, despite its aggressive presentation, has a relatively favorable prognosis compared to other AML subtypes, especially with targeted therapy using all-trans retinoic acid (ATRA) and arsenic trioxide (ATO). However, the question asks for the *associated* prognostic factor, and t(15;17) itself is the defining characteristic of APL, which historically had a poor prognosis before targeted therapies. In the context of modern treatment, it's considered favorable, but the translocation itself is the key diagnostic and prognostic marker. The question asks for the *most likely diagnosis* and *associated prognostic factor*. The translocation t(15;17) is the defining feature of APL (a subtype of AML) and is intrinsically linked to its specific biology and response to therapy. While modern therapy makes the prognosis good, the translocation itself is the critical factor.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. While t(8;21) is associated with AML (specifically AML with maturation, M2), it is not the translocation found in this patient. t(8;21) is associated with a favorable prognosis. The patient has t(15;17).
- (B): Incorrect. t(12;21) is associated with ALL (specifically pre-B ALL), not AML. The patient's presentation has features suggestive of both ALL (lymphoblast morphology) and AML (Auer rods, t(15;17)). However, the t(15;17) translocation definitively identifies the leukemia as APL (AML).
- (D): Incorrect. t(9;22) (Philadelphia chromosome, BCR-ABL fusion) is associated with Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and can also occur in ALL (Ph+ ALL) or rarely AML. It is associated with a poor prognosis in ALL. The patient has t(15;17).
- (E): Incorrect. inv(16) or t(16;16) is associated with AML with abnormal eosinophils (M4Eo subtype). It is associated with a favorable prognosis. The patient has t(15;17).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL) / AML M3 | AML with t(8;21) | ALL with t(12;21) | Ph+ ALL/CML | AML with inv(16) |
|----------------------|-------------------------------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-------------|-------------------|
| **Diagnosis**        | AML (APL)                                  | AML             | ALL             | ALL/CML     | AML               |
| **Cytogenetics**     | t(15;17)(q24;q21)                          | t(8;21)(q22;q22) | t(12;21)(p13;q22)| t(9;22)     | inv(16)(p13.1q22) |
| **Fusion Gene**      | PML-RARA                                   | RUNX1-RUNX1T1   | ETV6-RUNX1      | BCR-ABL     | CBFB-MYH11        |
| **Morphology**       | Abnormal promyelocytes, Auer rods          | Myeloblasts     | Lymphoblasts    | Blasts      | Eosinophils, Blasts|
| **Prognosis (Modern)**| Favorable (ATRA/ATO)                      | Favorable       | Favorable       | Poor        | Favorable         |
| **Key Feature**      | Specific translocation, differentiation block | Specific translocation | Specific translocation | Specific translocation | Specific translocation |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The presence of the t(15;17) translocation is pathognomonic for Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML. While Auer rods can be seen in AML, the specific translocation overrides morphological ambiguity. APL has a distinct pathophysiology involving the PML-RARA fusion protein blocking myeloid differentiation and is uniquely responsive to ATRA and ATO, leading to a favorable prognosis with modern therapy. Differentiating leukemias based on cytogenetics is crucial for diagnosis and treatment planning. (t(15;17) 易错点：虽然外周血涂片提示 ALL (lymphoblast morphology)，但 Auer rods 和 t(15;17) 明确诊断为 APL/AML。)

<a id='question-126'></a>

### Question 126: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-26 (⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating acute myeloid leukemia (AML) subtypes based on cytogenetics and morphology, specifically recognizing the prognostic significance of t(8;21) translocation and its association with AML with maturation (M2).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent fevers. His past medical history is significant for hypertension and hyperlipidemia. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the upper extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 45,000/µL) with a predominance of immature myeloid cells (blasts 40%), anemia (Hgb 8 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 25,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows myeloid cells at various stages of maturation, including promyelocytes, myelocytes, metamyelocytes, and bands, along with Auer rods in some blasts. Bone marrow biopsy confirms the diagnosis of acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22).

Which of the following is the most likely classification and associated prognostic implication for this patient's AML?

(A) Acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL) with t(15;17), associated with a poor prognosis due to high relapse rates.
(B) AML with maturation (M2) with t(8;21), associated with a favorable prognosis due to responsiveness to standard chemotherapy.
(C) Acute monoblastic/monocytic leukemia (M5) with inv(16) or t(16;16), associated with a poor prognosis due to resistance to cytarabine.
(D) AML with minimal differentiation (M0) with t(8;21), associated with a poor prognosis due to aggressive disease and frequent relapse.
(E) Acute megakaryoblastic leukemia (M7) with t(1;22), associated with a variable prognosis depending on age and cytogenetics.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, fevers, pallor, petechiae, splenomegaly. These are classic signs and symptoms of acute leukemia due to bone marrow failure (anemia, thrombocytopenia) and infiltration by leukemic blasts.
- **CBC Results:** Leukocytosis (45,000/µL) with 40% blasts, anemia (8 g/dL), thrombocytopenia (25,000/µL). Confirms pancytopenia with leukemic predominance.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Myeloid cells at various stages of maturation (promyelocytes, myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands) plus Auer rods in blasts. This indicates a myeloid lineage leukemia with evidence of maturation, ruling out M0 (minimal differentiation) and suggesting AML with maturation (M2). Auer rods are characteristic of AML, particularly M2 and M3.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Confirms AML.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;21)(q22;q22). This specific translocation is the defining cytogenetic abnormality for AML with maturation (M2).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the classification and prognostic implication based on the provided information.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **AML Classification:** The French-American-British (FAB) classification system (M0-M7) is based on morphology and maturation. The World Health Organization (WHO) classification integrates morphology, immunophenotype, cytogenetics, and molecular genetics.
- **AML with Maturation (M2):** Characterized by the presence of myeloid precursors at various stages of maturation in the bone marrow and peripheral blood. Auer rods are often present. The most common cytogenetic abnormality associated with M2 is the translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22), resulting in the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion gene.
- **t(8;21) Prognosis:** This translocation is generally associated with a favorable prognosis in AML, as these patients often respond well to standard induction chemotherapy (e.g., cytarabine and an anthracycline).
- **Other AML Subtypes & Cytogenetics:**
    - **APL (M3):** Characterized by abnormal promyelocytes and t(15;17)(q24;q21), creating the PML-RARA fusion gene. It has a distinct clinical presentation (often DIC) and treatment (ATRA, arsenic trioxide). Prognosis is generally good with specific therapy.
    - **AML M5:** Characterized by monoblasts/monocytes. Associated cytogenetics can include inv(16) or t(16;16) (CBFB-MYH11 fusion), which is associated with a poor prognosis.
    - **AML M0:** Characterized by undifferentiated blasts lacking myeloid differentiation markers. Prognosis is generally poor.
    - **AML M7:** Characterized by megakaryoblasts. Associated cytogenetics can include t(1;22)(p13;q12) (RARA-PP2AF1 fusion), which is associated with a poor prognosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. The translocation t(15;17) is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL, M3), not AML with maturation. While APL has a good prognosis with specific treatment (ATRA, arsenic), the translocation presented is t(8;21).
- (C): Incorrect. inv(16) or t(16;16) is associated with Acute Monoblastic/Monocytic Leukemia (M5), not AML with maturation (M2). Furthermore, this specific abnormality is associated with a poor prognosis, contrary to the favorable prognosis of t(8;21).
- (D): Incorrect. AML with minimal differentiation (M0) is characterized by a lack of maturation. While t(8;21) is associated with AML with maturation (M2), M0 typically has a poor prognosis. The morphology described (various stages of maturation) contradicts M0.
- (E): Incorrect. Acute megakaryoblastic leukemia (M7) is characterized by megakaryoblasts. The translocation t(1;22) is associated with M7, but the patient's morphology (various myeloid stages) and the given translocation t(8;21) are inconsistent with M7.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | AML with Maturation (M2) + t(8;21) | APL (M3) + t(15;17) | AML M5 + inv(16) | AML M0 | AML M7 + t(1;22) |
|----------------------|-----------------------------------|----------------------|-------------------|--------|-------------------|
| **Morphology**       | Myeloid maturation present        | Abnormal promyelocytes | Monoblasts/Monocytes | Blasts, no maturation | Megakaryoblasts |
| **Auer Rods**        | Often present                     | Rare                 | Rare              | Rare  | Rare              |
| **Cytogenetics**     | t(8;21)                           | t(15;17)             | inv(16)/t(16;16)  | Variable | t(1;22)           |
| **Fusion Gene**      | RUNX1-RUNX1T1                     | PML-RARA             | CBFB-MYH11       | N/A    | RARA-PP2AF1       |
| **Prognosis**        | Favorable                         | Good (w/ ATRA)       | Poor              | Poor  | Poor              |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The presence of myeloid maturation (promyelocytes, myelocytes, etc.) and Auer rods in blasts, combined with the specific cytogenetic finding of t(8;21), definitively points to AML with maturation (M2). This subtype is associated with a favorable prognosis compared to other AML subtypes like M0, M3 (without ATRA), M5 (with inv(16)), or M7. Remember the key cytogenetic abnormalities and their associated AML subtypes and prognoses.
