<a id='question-127'></a>

### Question 127: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-27 (⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of specific cytogenetic abnormalities and their association with distinct types of acute myeloid leukemia (AML), particularly the prognostic significance of t(8;21) and inv(16) versus t(15;17) and other high-risk features. (识别特定的细胞遗传学异常及其与不同类型急性髓系白血病 (AML) 的关联，特别是 t(8;21) 和 inv(16) 与 t(15;17) 以及其他高危特征的预后意义。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent fevers. He has no significant past medical history. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the upper extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 85,000/µL) with a predominance of blasts, severe anemia (Hgb 7.5 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 15,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows medium-sized cells with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and moderate cytoplasm containing Auer rods. Bone marrow biopsy confirms acute myeloid leukemia (AML) with 80% blasts. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22). Which of the following is the MOST likely clinical course and associated prognostic factor for this patient?

(A) Rapidly progressive disease with poor response to standard chemotherapy, associated with the presence of MLL gene rearrangements.
(B) Favorable prognosis with high rates of complete remission and long-term survival, associated with the fusion gene RUNX1-RUNX1T1.
(C) Intermediate prognosis with variable response to therapy, associated with the presence of NPM1 mutations.
(D) Aggressive disease characterized by extramedullary involvement and poor survival, associated with the translocation t(15;17)(q24;q21).
(E) Poor prognosis with a high risk of relapse, associated with complex karyotype abnormalities including monosomy 7.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, fevers, pallor, petechiae, splenomegaly. These are classic symptoms and signs of acute leukemia due to bone marrow failure (anemia, thrombocytopenia) and infiltration by leukemic blasts.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Leukocytosis with blast predominance (WBC 85,000/µL, 80% blasts in bone marrow), severe anemia (Hgb 7.5 g/dL), thrombocytopenia (platelets 15,000/µL). These confirm the diagnosis of acute leukemia.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Medium-sized cells, fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, moderate cytoplasm, Auer rods. These morphological features are characteristic of myeloblasts, strongly suggesting Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). Auer rods are particularly indicative of myeloid lineage.
- **Cytogenetic Analysis:** t(8;21)(q22;q22). This is the critical piece of information. This specific translocation is a recurrent genetic abnormality in AML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely clinical course and associated prognostic factor. This requires linking the specific cytogenetic abnormality (t(8;21)) to its known prognostic implications in AML.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **AML Classification:** AML is classified based on morphology, cytogenetics, and molecular features. Cytogenetic abnormalities are major determinants of prognosis.
- **t(8;21)(q22;q22):** This translocation involves the *RUNX1* gene on chromosome 8 and the *RUNX1T1* (formerly *AML1*) gene on chromosome 21. The translocation creates a fusion gene, *RUNX1-RUNX1T1*, which encodes a transcription factor that disrupts normal hematopoiesis.
- **Prognostic Significance of t(8;21):** AML with t(8;21) is classified as having a *favorable* prognosis according to the World Health Organization (WHO) classification and the European LeukemiaNet (ELN) risk stratification. Patients with this abnormality typically have high rates of complete remission after standard induction chemotherapy (e.g., cytarabine and an anthracycline) and achieve long-term disease-free survival.
- **Other Cytogenetic Abnormalities:**
    - **t(15;17)(q24;q21):** This translocation involves the *PML* gene on chromosome 15 and the *RARA* gene on chromosome 17, creating the *PML-RARA* fusion gene. It is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML with a distinct clinical presentation (often DIC) and treatment (ATRA, arsenic trioxide). While APL is often curable, t(15;17) itself is not considered a "favorable" prognostic factor in the general context of AML risk stratification compared to t(8;21).
    - **MLL gene rearrangements (e.g., t(6;11)(q27;q23)):** These are associated with *poor* prognosis, particularly in younger patients.
    - **NPM1 mutations:** These are common in AML and are generally associated with a *favorable* prognosis, especially in the absence of *FLT3-ITD* mutations. However, t(8;21) is considered one of the most favorable cytogenetic abnormalities.
    - **Complex karyotype (≥3 abnormalities) or Monosomy 7:** These are associated with *poor* prognosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. MLL gene rearrangements (e.g., t(6;11)) are associated with a *poor* prognosis, not a favorable one. The clinical course is typically aggressive.
- (C): Incorrect. NPM1 mutations are generally associated with a *favorable* prognosis, but t(8;21) is considered a more specific and strongly favorable cytogenetic marker. The question asks for the factor *associated* with the specific finding (t(8;21)).
- (D): Incorrect. t(15;17) is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), which has a distinct clinical presentation and treatment, and while often curable, it's not the abnormality found in this patient (t(8;21)). Furthermore, APL is not typically described as having aggressive disease characterized by extramedullary involvement in the same way as some high-risk AML subtypes.
- (E): Incorrect. Complex karyotype and monosomy 7 are associated with a *poor* prognosis, not a favorable one.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | t(8;21) AML (RUNX1-RUNX1T1) | t(15;17) AML (APL, PML-RARA) | MLL Rearrangement AML | NPM1 Mutation AML | Complex Karyotype/Monosomy 7 AML |
|----------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|------------------------|--------------------|-----------------------------------|
| **Cytogenetic/Gene** | t(8;21), RUNX1-RUNX1T1      | t(15;17), PML-RARA           | Various (e.g., t(6;11)) | NPM1 mutation     | ≥3 abnormalities, Monosomy 7     |
| **Prognosis**        | Favorable                   | Often Curable (APL specific) | Poor                   | Favorable          | Poor                               |
| **Clinical Features**| Typical AML                 | Often DIC, high WBC         | Variable               | Variable           | Variable, often refractory        |
| **Treatment**        | Standard AML chemo          | ATRA, Arsenic Trioxide       | Intensive chemo       | Standard AML chemo | Intensive chemo, often poor response |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22), resulting in the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion gene, is a hallmark of a specific subtype of AML associated with a favorable prognosis and high cure rates with standard chemotherapy. Differentiating this favorable cytogenetic abnormality from high-risk ones like MLL rearrangements, complex karyotypes, or monosomy 7 is crucial for predicting patient outcome and guiding treatment intensity. Remember that t(15;17) defines APL, a distinct entity with specific treatment.

<a id='question-128'></a>

### Question 128: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-28 (⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑⯑)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiation between Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) subtypes based on cytogenetics and morphology, specifically distinguishing AML with t(8;21) from other AML variants like AML with inv(16) or acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL). (区分AML亚型，基于细胞遗传学和形态学，特别是t(8;21) AML与其他AML变体，如inv(16) AML或APL的鉴别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent nosebleeds. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the upper extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 45,000/µL) with a predominance of blasts (80%), anemia (Hgb 8 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 20,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows medium-sized blasts with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and moderate cytoplasm containing Auer rods. Bone marrow aspirate confirms the diagnosis of acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow reveals a translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22).

Which of the following is the most likely prognosis and associated characteristic for this patient's specific AML subtype?

(A) Poor prognosis, associated with inv(16)(p13.1q22) and MML fusion gene.
(B) Intermediate prognosis, associated with t(15;17)(q24;q21) and PML-RARA fusion gene.
(C) Favorable prognosis, associated with t(8;21)(q22;q22) and RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion gene.
(D) Poor prognosis, associated with complex karyotype (multiple chromosomal abnormalities) and resistance to standard chemotherapy.
(E) Intermediate prognosis, associated with NPM1 mutations and FLT3-ITD mutations.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 55-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, nosebleeds (symptoms of bone marrow failure: anemia, thrombocytopenia).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), mild splenomegaly (common in leukemia).
- **CBC:** Leukocytosis with blast predominance (80%), anemia, thrombocytopenia – consistent with acute leukemia.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Medium-sized blasts, fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, moderate cytoplasm, Auer rods – characteristic features of AML blasts. Auer rods are particularly common in AML with t(8;21) and inv(16).
- **Bone Marrow:** Confirms AML diagnosis.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;21)(q22;q22) – This is the key diagnostic finding that defines a specific subtype of AML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the prognosis and associated characteristic of this specific cytogenetic abnormality.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **AML Subtypes:** AML is classified based on morphology (FAB classification) and cytogenetics/molecular genetics (WHO classification). Cytogenetic abnormalities are crucial for risk stratification and prognosis.
- **t(8;21)(q22;q22):** This translocation involves the *RUNX1* gene on chromosome 8 and the *RUNX1T1* gene (formerly *AML1*) on chromosome 21. The resulting fusion gene, *RUNX1-RUNX1T1*, encodes a transcription factor that disrupts normal hematopoiesis, leading to the proliferation of myeloid blasts.
- **Prognosis:** AML with t(8;21) is generally associated with a *favorable* prognosis compared to other AML subtypes, particularly those with normal cytogenetics or adverse cytogenetic abnormalities. Patients often respond well to standard chemotherapy (e.g., cytarabine and anthracycline).
- **Associated Features:** Auer rods are frequently seen in blasts from patients with t(8;21) AML. The blasts are typically medium-sized with fine chromatin.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Poor prognosis, associated with inv(16)(p13.1q22) and MML fusion gene. This describes AML with inv(16), which is also associated with a *favorable* prognosis, not poor. The fusion gene is *CBFB-MYH11* (formerly MML). The patient has t(8;21), not inv(16).
- (B): Intermediate prognosis, associated with t(15;17)(q24;q21) and PML-RARA fusion gene. This describes Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML. APL has a *favorable* prognosis, especially with targeted therapy (ATRA, arsenic trioxide), but it is characterized by t(15;17) and the PML-RARA fusion, not t(8;21).
- (D): Poor prognosis, associated with complex karyotype (multiple chromosomal abnormalities) and resistance to standard chemotherapy. A complex karyotype (≥3 abnormalities) is associated with a *poor* prognosis. The patient has a specific, recurrent translocation (t(8;21)), not a complex karyotype.
- (E): Intermediate prognosis, associated with NPM1 mutations and FLT3-ITD mutations. NPM1 mutations are generally associated with a *favorable* prognosis in AML with normal cytogenetics. FLT3-ITD mutations are associated with an *intermediate to poor* prognosis, depending on the allelic ratio. While these mutations can occur in AML, they are not specifically linked to t(8;21), and the overall prognosis associated with t(8;21) is favorable.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | t(8;21) AML (Correct Answer) | inv(16) AML | t(15;17) APL | Complex Karyotype | NPM1 mut / FLT3-ITD mut |
|----------------------|-------------------------------|-------------|--------------|-------------------|---------------------------|
| **Cytogenetics**     | t(8;21)(q22;q22)               | inv(16)     | t(15;17)     | ≥3 abnormalities | Variable                  |
| **Fusion Gene**      | RUNX1-RUNX1T1                  | CBFB-MYH11  | PML-RARA     | N/A               | N/A                       |
| **Auer Rods**        | Common                        | Common      | Rare         | Variable          | Variable                  |
| **Prognosis**        | Favorable                     | Favorable   | Favorable    | Poor              | Intermediate/Poor         |
| **Key Association**  | RUNX1-RUNX1T1                 | CBFB-MYH11  | PML-RARA     | Resistance        | Prognostic markers        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The presence of t(8;21) in AML defines a specific subtype associated with a favorable prognosis due to the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion gene. This must be differentiated from other common AML cytogenetic abnormalities like inv(16) (favorable, CBFB-MYH11), t(15;17) (APL, favorable, PML-RARA), and complex karyotypes (poor prognosis). Remember that Auer rods are often seen in t(8;21) and inv(16) AML.

<a id='question-129'></a>

### Question 129: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiation between acute myeloid leukemia (AML) subtypes based on cytogenetic abnormalities and their prognostic significance, specifically focusing on t(8;21) and inv(16) as favorable risk factors versus abnormalities like t(15;17) associated with acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL) and its specific treatment implications. (区分AML亚型，基于细胞遗传学异常及其预后意义，特别是t(8;21)和inv(16)作为有利风险因素，以及t(15;17)与APL相关及其特定治疗意义。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent nosebleeds. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the upper extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 85,000/µL) with a predominance of immature myeloid cells (blasts 60%), anemia (Hgb 8 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 20,000/µL). Bone marrow aspirate and biopsy confirm the diagnosis of acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22.1). Which of the following is the MOST likely clinical course and appropriate initial management strategy for this patient?

(A) Rapidly progressive disease with high risk of early death due to disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC); immediate administration of all-trans retinoic acid (ATRA) and arsenic trioxide (ATO) is indicated.
(B) A subtype of AML associated with a poor prognosis; induction chemotherapy with cytarabine and daunorubicin is standard, but response rates are typically lower.
(C) A subtype of AML associated with a favorable prognosis; induction chemotherapy with cytarabine and an anthracycline (e.g., daunorubicin or idarubicin) is the standard approach.
(D) A subtype of AML associated with a poor prognosis; induction chemotherapy with high-dose cytarabine is indicated due to the presence of blasts with specific cytogenetic abnormalities.
(E) A subtype of AML associated with a favorable prognosis; treatment with ATRA and ATO is the standard initial therapy, followed by consolidation chemotherapy.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 55-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, nosebleeds (symptoms of bone marrow failure: anemia, thrombocytopenia).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), mild splenomegaly (common in leukemia).
- **CBC:** Leukocytosis with high blast count (60%), anemia, thrombocytopenia – consistent with acute leukemia.
- **Bone Marrow:** Confirms AML.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;21)(q22;q22.1) – This is the crucial finding. This translocation involves the *RUNX1T1* gene (formerly *AML1*) on chromosome 21 and the *RUNX1* gene (formerly *AML1-ETO*) on chromosome 8. This specific translocation is associated with a subtype of AML known as AML with t(8;21), which is classified as having a favorable prognosis.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most likely clinical course and appropriate initial management.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **AML Subtypes & Prognosis:** AML is heterogeneous. Cytogenetic abnormalities are major determinants of prognosis.
- **t(8;21):** This translocation creates the *RUNX1-RUNX1T1* fusion protein. This fusion protein interferes with normal myeloid differentiation, leading to the accumulation of immature myeloid cells (blasts). Despite its oncogenic role, AML with t(8;21) generally responds well to standard chemotherapy and has a relatively favorable prognosis compared to other AML subtypes.
- **Standard AML Treatment:** The standard initial treatment for most AML subtypes (including those with favorable cytogenetics like t(8;21) and inv(16)) is induction chemotherapy, typically involving cytarabine (ara-C) and an anthracycline (daunorubicin or idarubicin).
- **APL (t(15;17)):** Acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL) is a distinct subtype of AML characterized by the t(15;17) translocation, involving the *PML* and *RARA* genes. APL is associated with a high risk of DIC. The standard treatment for APL is ATRA and ATO, which induce differentiation of the leukemic promyelocytes, rather than standard chemotherapy.
- **Other AML Cytogenetics:** Other common cytogenetic abnormalities include inv(16) or t(16;16) (favorable prognosis), t(15;17) (APL, requires ATRA/ATO), abnormalities of chromosome 3 (poor prognosis), monosomy 5 or del(5q) (poor prognosis), monosomy 7 or del(7q) (poor prognosis), complex karyotype (poor prognosis).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): This describes the clinical course and treatment for APL (t(15;17)), characterized by a high risk of DIC and treatment with ATRA and ATO. The patient's cytogenetics are t(8;21), not t(15;17). DIC is not a characteristic feature of AML with t(8;21).
- (B): This describes a poor prognosis associated with t(8;21). AML with t(8;21) is actually associated with a *favorable* prognosis. Standard induction chemotherapy is appropriate, but the prognosis is good.
- (D): This describes a poor prognosis and the use of high-dose cytarabine. While high-dose cytarabine might be used in certain AML subtypes or relapsed disease, AML with t(8;21) has a favorable prognosis, and standard-dose chemotherapy is typically sufficient for induction. The prognosis is not poor.
- (E): This describes the treatment for APL (ATRA and ATO) and states it has a favorable prognosis. While APL treatment involves ATRA/ATO and has a good prognosis *with appropriate therapy*, the patient has t(8;21), not t(15;17), and therefore requires standard chemotherapy, not ATRA/ATO.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | AML with t(8;21)                     | Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL) t(15;17) |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|
| Cytogenetics        | t(8;21)(q22;q22.1)                    | t(15;17)(q24.1;q21.2)                      |
| Fusion Gene         | RUNX1-RUNX1T1                         | PML-RARA                                  |
| Prognosis           | Favorable                             | Good (with specific therapy)               |
| Key Complication    | None specific                         | High risk of DIC                           |
| Standard Treatment  | Cytarabine + Anthracycline            | ATRA + ATO                                 |
| Morphologic Features| Myeloblasts, Auer rods possible       | Promyelocytes, Auer rods (often numerous)  |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
AML classification relies heavily on cytogenetics for prognosis and treatment planning. t(8;21) and inv(16) are associated with a favorable prognosis and respond well to standard induction chemotherapy (cytarabine + anthracycline). t(15;17) defines APL, which requires specific treatment with ATRA and ATO due to the high risk of DIC. Other cytogenetic abnormalities are associated with intermediate or poor prognoses. Key differentials include distinguishing AML subtypes based on cytogenetics and their corresponding prognoses and treatments.

<a id='question-130'></a>

### Question 130: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-30 (Burkitt Lymphoma)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the recognition of Burkitt lymphoma based on clinical presentation (rapidly growing mass, often extranodal), characteristic histopathology (starry sky appearance), and the underlying genetic translocation involving the *MYC* oncogene. (本题考察伯基特淋巴瘤的识别，依据临床表现（快速生长的肿块，常为结外）、特征性组织病理学（星空样外观）以及潜在的遗传易位，涉及 *MYC* 癌基因。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 12-year-old boy from a rural area in equatorial Africa presents to the clinic with a rapidly enlarging mass in his jaw, causing facial asymmetry and difficulty chewing. The mass has been growing for approximately 3 months. He also reports intermittent fevers and night sweats. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass measuring 8 cm in diameter involving the mandible. Laboratory tests show elevated serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) and uric acid levels. A biopsy of the jaw mass is performed. Histopathological examination reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. Scattered macrophages containing phagocytosed cellular debris are observed throughout the tumor infiltrate, creating a characteristic "starry sky" appearance. Immunohistochemical staining shows the tumor cells are positive for CD20 and CD10, and negative for CD5. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving chromosome 8 and chromosome 14.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying genetic abnormality responsible for this patient's condition?

(A) t(11;14)(q13;q32)
(B) t(8;14)(q24;q32)
(C) t(9;22)(q34;q11)
(D) t(14;18)(q32;q21)
(E) t(15;17)(q24;q21)

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 12-year-old boy from equatorial Africa. This demographic is highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection in this region.
- **Chief Complaint:** Rapidly growing jaw mass (mandible). This is a classic presentation for endemic Burkitt lymphoma, often presenting as a rapidly expanding tumor in the jaw or other extranodal sites.
- **Symptoms:** Intermittent fevers and night sweats (B symptoms). These are systemic symptoms often associated with lymphomas.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender mass (8 cm) in the mandible. Confirms the rapidly growing nature and location.
- **Labs:** Elevated LDH and uric acid. High LDH is common in rapidly proliferating tumors like Burkitt lymphoma due to high cell turnover. Elevated uric acid can result from tumor lysis syndrome, which can occur with rapid cell death.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, and a "starry sky" appearance (macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic cells). This is the pathognomonic histological feature of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ and CD10+, CD5-. This immunophenotype is characteristic of B-cell lymphomas, specifically Burkitt lymphoma (which is a mature B-cell lymphoma). CD5 negativity helps distinguish it from chronic lymphocytic leukemia/small lymphocytic lymphoma (CLL/SLL) and mantle cell lymphoma.
- **Cytogenetics:** Translocation involving chromosome 8 and chromosome 14. This is the key genetic hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Its pathogenesis is driven by a specific genetic translocation that leads to the overexpression of the *MYC* oncogene.
- **The t(8;14)(q24;q32) Translocation:** This translocation juxtaposes the *MYC* gene (located on chromosome 8q24) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus (located on chromosome 14q32). The IgH locus contains strong enhancer elements that drive high levels of transcription. This leads to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein.
- **MYC Protein Function:** MYC is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. Overexpression of MYC drives uncontrolled cell proliferation, characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Endemic vs. Sporadic Burkitt Lymphoma:** While t(8;14) is the most common translocation (~80%), variants exist. Endemic Burkitt lymphoma (associated with EBV) typically involves t(8;14). Sporadic Burkitt lymphoma can involve t(8;14), t(2;8)(p12;q24) (involving the kappa light chain locus), or t(8;22)(q24;q11) (involving the lambda light chain locus). All these translocations result in *MYC* deregulation.
- **Clinical Correlation:** The rapid growth, high proliferation rate (reflected by high LDH), and characteristic histology/immunophenotype described in the vignette are all consistent with Burkitt lymphoma driven by *MYC* overexpression due to the t(8;14) translocation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) t(11;14)(q13;q32): This translocation involves the *CCND1* gene (encoding cyclin D1) on chromosome 11q13 and the IgH locus on chromosome 14q32. It is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma. Mantle cell lymphoma typically presents in older adults, involves lymph nodes, spleen, and bone marrow, and has a different immunophenotype (CD5+, CD20+).
- (B) t(8;14)(q24;q32): This translocation involves the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8q24 and the IgH locus on chromosome 14q32. This is the classic translocation found in the majority (~80%) of Burkitt lymphoma cases, leading to *MYC* overexpression and uncontrolled proliferation. This matches the clinical and pathological findings in the vignette.
- (C) t(9;22)(q34;q11): This translocation, known as the Philadelphia chromosome, involves the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22q11 and the *ABL1* gene on chromosome 9q34. It results in the formation of the *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene, which encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. This translocation is the hallmark of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and is also found in some cases of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). The clinical presentation and pathology described are not consistent with CML or ALL.
- (D) t(14;18)(q32;q21): This translocation involves the IgH locus on chromosome 14q32 and the *BCL2* gene on chromosome 18q21. It leads to overexpression of the BCL2 protein, an anti-apoptotic protein. This translocation is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma. Follicular lymphoma typically presents as indolent lymphadenopathy, has a different histology (follicular pattern), and immunophenotype (often CD10+, BCL6+).
- (E) t(15;17)(q24;q21): This translocation involves the *PML* gene on chromosome 15q24 and the *RARA* gene on chromosome 17q21. It results in the formation of the *PML-RARA* fusion gene, which encodes a fusion protein with aberrant transcriptional activity. This translocation is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). APL presents with pancytopenia, coagulopathy (DIC), and characteristic promyelocytes in the bone marrow/blood. The clinical presentation and pathology described are not consistent with APL.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14)) | CML (t(9;22)) | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | APL (t(15;17)) |
|----------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------|----------------|---------------------------------|-----------------|
| **Translocation**    | t(8;14)(q24;q32)          | t(11;14)(q13;q32)               | t(9;22)(q34;q11)| t(14;18)(q32;q21)               | t(15;17)(q24;q21)|
| **Gene Involved**    | *MYC*, *IgH*              | *CCND1*, *IgH*                  | *BCR*, *ABL1*  | *BCL2*, *IgH*                  | *PML*, *RARA*   |
| **Cell Type**        | Mature B-cell             | Mature B-cell                   | Myeloid        | Mature B-cell                   | Myeloid         |
| **Typical Age**      | Children/Young Adults     | Older Adults                    | Middle Age     | Middle Age/Older Adults          | Adults          |
| **Clinical Feature** | Rapidly growing mass      | Lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly    | Leukocytosis   | Indolent lymphadenopathy        | Pancytopenia, DIC|
| **Histology**        | Starry sky                | Mantle zone pattern             | Hypercellular BM| Follicular pattern              | Promyelocytes   |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, CD5-        | CD20+, CD5+, CD23-              | CD34+, CD117+  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+             | CD34+, CD117+   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by a specific translocation, most commonly t(8;14), leading to *MYC* overexpression and rapid proliferation. Remember the classic "starry sky" histology and association with EBV in endemic forms. Differentiate it from other lymphomas based on translocation partners (t(11;14) for Mantle Cell, t(14;18) for Follicular), clinical presentation, and immunophenotype. (伯基特淋巴瘤的特征是特定的易位，最常见的是 t(8;14)，导致 *MYC* 过度表达和快速增殖。记住经典的“星空样”组织学和内源性形式与 EB 病毒的关联。根据易位伴侣（Mantle Cell 的 t(11;14)，Follicular 的 t(14;18)）、临床表现和免疫表型与其他淋巴瘤相鉴别。)

<a id='question-131'></a>

### Question 131: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiation between acute myeloid leukemia (AML) subtypes based on cytogenetic abnormalities and their prognostic significance, specifically focusing on t(8;21) translocation and its association with a favorable prognosis. (区分急性髓系白血病亚型，基于细胞遗传学异常及其预后意义，特别是t(8;21)易位及其与良好预后的关联。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent nosebleeds. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the upper extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 45,000/µL) with a predominance of blasts (80%), anemia (Hgb 8 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 20,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows medium-sized cells with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and Auer rods. Bone marrow biopsy confirms hypercellularity with 85% blasts. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals a translocation between chromosomes 8 and 21, designated t(8;21)(q22;q22.1). Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) confirms the presence of the *RUNX1-RUNX1T1* fusion gene. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis and associated prognosis?

(A) Acute myeloid leukemia with t(15;17), associated with a poor prognosis.
(B) Acute myeloid leukemia with inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22), associated with a poor prognosis.
(C) Acute myeloid leukemia with t(8;21)(q22;q22.1), associated with a favorable prognosis.
(D) Acute promyelocytic leukemia with t(15;17)(q24.1;q21.2), associated with a favorable prognosis.
(E) Acute myeloid leukemia with del(5q) or monosomy 5, associated with a favorable prognosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, nosebleeds (symptoms of bone marrow failure: anemia, thrombocytopenia).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), mild splenomegaly (common in leukemias).
- **CBC:** Leukocytosis with blast predominance (80%), anemia (Hgb 8 g/dL), thrombocytopenia (platelets 20,000/µL) - consistent with acute leukemia.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Medium-sized blasts, fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, Auer rods - characteristic features of myeloblasts, strongly suggesting Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). Auer rods are pathognomonic for AML.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Hypercellularity with 85% blasts - confirms the diagnosis of acute leukemia.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;21)(q22;q22.1) - This specific translocation is a key diagnostic feature.
- **FISH:** Confirms *RUNX1-RUNX1T1* fusion gene - This gene results from the t(8;21) translocation.
- **Question:** Asks for the diagnosis and associated prognosis based on the cytogenetic findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **AML Diagnosis:** The clinical presentation, CBC, peripheral smear (blasts with Auer rods), and bone marrow biopsy (85% blasts) definitively diagnose Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML).
- **Cytogenetic Abnormalities in AML:** Specific chromosomal translocations and abnormalities are crucial for classifying AML subtypes and predicting prognosis.
- **t(8;21)(q22;q22.1):** This translocation involves the *RUNX1* gene on chromosome 21 and the *RUNX1T1* gene (formerly *AML1*) on chromosome 8. It results in the *RUNX1-RUNX1T1* fusion gene. This fusion protein disrupts normal hematopoiesis, leading to the proliferation of myeloid blasts.
- **Prognostic Significance of t(8;21):** AML with t(8;21) is classified as a core-binding factor (CBF) leukemia. These leukemias generally have a favorable prognosis compared to AML with normal cytogenetics or other adverse cytogenetic abnormalities. Patients often respond well to standard chemotherapy regimens (e.g., cytarabine and anthracycline).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **t(15;17)** is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL). It involves the *PML* gene on chromosome 15 and the *RARA* gene on chromosome 17, creating the *PML-RARA* fusion protein. APL is a distinct subtype of AML, but it is associated with a *favorable* prognosis, especially with targeted therapy (ATRA, arsenic trioxide). The vignette describes Auer rods, which are less common in APL. The translocation is incorrect.
- (B): **inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22)** involves the *CBFB* gene on chromosome 16. This is another core-binding factor (CBF) leukemia, similar to t(8;21), and is also associated with a *favorable* prognosis. However, the vignette specifically states the presence of t(8;21), making this option incorrect despite the favorable prognosis association.
- (D): **t(15;17)(q24.1;q21.2)** is the translocation associated with Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), as described in (A). APL has a *favorable* prognosis, not poor. The translocation is incorrect for the vignette.
- (E): **del(5q) or monosomy 5** is considered an adverse cytogenetic abnormality in AML and is associated with a *poor* prognosis. The vignette describes t(8;21), which is associated with a favorable prognosis.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | t(8;21) AML (Correct) | t(15;17) APL (Incorrect) | inv(16) AML (Incorrect) | del(5q)/Monosomy 5 AML (Incorrect) |
|----------------------|-----------------------|--------------------------|-------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| **Translocation**   | t(8;21)(q22;q22.1)    | t(15;17)(q24.1;q21.2)    | inv(16)(p13.1q22)      | del(5q) or Monosomy 5             |
| **Fusion Gene**     | RUNX1-RUNX1T1         | PML-RARA                 | CBFB-MYH11              | N/A                               |
| **Auer Rods**        | Common                | Uncommon                 | Common                  | Common                            |
| **Prognosis**        | Favorable             | Favorable                | Favorable               | Poor                              |
| **Specific Features**| Often hypogranular blasts | Hypergranular blasts, DIC | Often eosinophilia      | Often refractory to therapy       |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The presence of t(8;21) in AML, resulting in the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion gene, is a key cytogenetic marker associated with a favorable prognosis. Differentiating AML subtypes based on cytogenetics is crucial for risk stratification and treatment planning. Remember that t(15;17) defines APL (favorable prognosis), inv(16) is also favorable, and del(5q)/monosomy 5 is adverse.

<a id='question-132'></a>

### Question 132: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL) based on characteristic immunophenotype and genetic translocation, specifically t(11;14)(q13;q32) leading to cyclin D1 overexpression. (鉴别淋巴瘤类型，基于特征性免疫表型和遗传易位，特别是t(11;14)(q13;q32)导致D1环素过表达。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to his primary care physician with a 6-month history of progressive fatigue, unintentional weight loss (15 lbs), and night sweats. He denies fever or recent infections. His past medical history is significant for hypertension and hyperlipidemia, both well-controlled. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, including cervical, axillary, and inguinal nodes, which are firm and non-tender. Splenomegaly is also noted on abdominal examination. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 45,000/µL) with a predominance of small to medium-sized lymphocytes, mild anemia (Hgb 10.5 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 80,000/µL). A lymph node biopsy is performed. Histopathology shows effacement of the normal nodal architecture by sheets of small to medium-sized atypical lymphocytes with round nuclei, slightly irregular chromatin, and inconspicuous nucleoli. Immunohistochemistry reveals the neoplastic cells are positive for CD5, CD19, CD20, and cyclin D1, and negative for CD10 and CD23. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation t(11;14)(q13;q32).

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Follicular Lymphoma
(B) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL)
(C) Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL)
(D) Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL)
(E) Burkitt Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with B symptoms (fatigue, weight loss, night sweats), generalized lymphadenopathy, and splenomegaly. This suggests a lymphoproliferative disorder.
- **CBC Findings:** Leukocytosis with lymphocytosis, anemia, and thrombocytopenia indicate bone marrow involvement and displacement of normal hematopoietic cells by the neoplastic process.
- **Histopathology:** Effacement of nodal architecture by small to medium-sized atypical lymphocytes points towards a lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry (IHC):**
    - CD19, CD20 positive: Confirms B-cell lineage.
    - CD5 positive: Narrows the differential to specific B-cell lymphomas, notably CLL/SLL and MCL.
    - CD10 negative: Helps differentiate from Follicular Lymphoma (typically CD10+).
    - CD23 negative: Helps differentiate from CLL/SLL (typically CD23+).
    - Cyclin D1 positive: This is a key marker for MCL.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(11;14)(q13;q32) is the characteristic translocation associated with MCL, leading to overexpression of the CCND1 gene (encoding cyclin D1).
- **Synthesis:** The combination of clinical presentation, B-cell immunophenotype (CD19+, CD20+, CD5+), negativity for CD10 and CD23, positivity for cyclin D1, and the specific t(11;14) translocation strongly points to Mantle Cell Lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL):** A mature B-cell lymphoma arising from the mantle zone of lymph nodes.
- **Pathogenesis:** Characterized by the t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation in ~95% of cases. This translocation juxtaposes the *CCND1* gene (encoding cyclin D1) on chromosome 11q13 with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) gene locus on chromosome 14q32.
- **Molecular Mechanism:** The IgH enhancer drives overexpression of cyclin D1. Cyclin D1 forms a complex with cyclin-dependent kinase 4 (CDK4) or CDK6. This complex phosphorylates the retinoblastoma protein (Rb), leading to the release of E2F transcription factors. E2F promotes the transcription of genes required for cell cycle progression from G1 to S phase, resulting in uncontrolled proliferation of the neoplastic B cells.
- **Immunophenotype:** Typically CD5+, CD19+, CD20+, CD22+, FMC7+, cyclin D1+, CD10-, CD23-. The CD5 positivity distinguishes it from most other B-cell lymphomas, except CLL/SLL. The cyclin D1 positivity and t(11;14) are highly specific for MCL.
- **Clinical Features:** Often presents with widespread lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, and bone marrow involvement (leading to cytopenias). B symptoms are common. It is generally an aggressive lymphoma with a poor prognosis compared to some other indolent lymphomas.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Follicular Lymphoma:** Typically presents with generalized lymphadenopathy. Histologically shows follicular growth pattern with centrocytes and centroblasts. Immunophenotype is usually CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+, CD5-, cyclin D1-. The t(14;18) translocation involving the *BCL2* gene is characteristic. This patient is CD10- and cyclin D1+, making Follicular Lymphoma unlikely.
- (B) **Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL):** The most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Presents with rapidly enlarging mass, often extranodal. Histologically shows diffuse sheets of large atypical B cells. Immunophenotype is CD19+, CD20+, CD10 variable, CD5 usually negative, cyclin D1 negative. While it can present similarly, the specific immunophenotype (CD5+, cyclin D1+) and translocation are not typical for DLBCL.
- (C) **Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL):** Characterized by the accumulation of mature-appearing small lymphocytes. Often presents with lymphocytosis and lymphadenopathy. Immunophenotype is typically CD5+, CD19+, CD20+ (dim), CD23+, CD10-, cyclin D1-. The presence of CD23 and absence of cyclin D1 positivity make CLL/SLL unlikely in this case.
- (E) **Burkitt Lymphoma:** A highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma. Histologically shows a "starry sky" pattern with medium-sized cells and numerous macrophages. Immunophenotype is CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2-, cyclin D1-. The characteristic translocation is t(8;14) involving the *MYC* gene. The immunophenotype (CD10+) and lack of cyclin D1 positivity rule out Burkitt Lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL) | Follicular Lymphoma | DLBCL             | CLL/SLL           | Burkitt Lymphoma |
|----------------------|-----------------------------|----------------------|-------------------|-------------------|------------------|
| **Age (Median)**     | ~60s                        | ~60s                 | ~60s              | ~70s              | ~30s             |
| **Presentation**     | Lymphadenopathy, B symptoms | Lymphadenopathy      | Rapid mass        | Lymphocytosis     | Rapid mass       |
| **Histology**        | Small-medium cells, diffuse | Follicular pattern   | Diffuse large cells | Small lymphocytes | Medium cells, starry sky |
| **CD5**              | +                           | -                    | -                 | +                 | -                |
| **CD10**             | -                           | +                    | Variable          | -                 | +                |
| **CD23**             | -                           | -                    | -                 | +                 | -                |
| **Cyclin D1**        | +                           | -                    | -                 | -                 | -                |
| **Characteristic t** | t(11;14)                    | t(14;18)             | Variable          | del(13q)          | t(8;14)          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Mantle Cell Lymphoma is characterized by the t(11;14) translocation leading to cyclin D1 overexpression, resulting in a CD5+, CD10-, CD23-, cyclin D1+ immunophenotype. Differentiating MCL from other CD5+ B-cell lymphomas like CLL/SLL relies on the absence of CD23 and the presence of cyclin D1 in MCL. Remember the key genetic translocation and its downstream effect on cell cycle regulation.

<a id='question-133'></a>

### Question 133: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-33 (arise from mantlezone B lymphocytes(positive for C)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of follicular lymphoma based on characteristic immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL2+) and morphology (centrocytes, centroblasts, tingible body macrophages), distinguishing it from other B-cell lymphomas like mantle cell lymphoma (CD5+, Cyclin D1+), diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (CD10 variable, BCL2 variable), and marginal zone lymphoma (CD79a+, CD5+/-).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old woman presents to her primary care physician with a 3-month history of painless swelling in her left neck. She denies fever, night sweats, or weight loss (B symptoms). Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender, rubbery mass approximately 5 cm in diameter in the left supraclavicular region. The remainder of the physical examination is unremarkable. An excisional lymph node biopsy is performed. Histopathology shows a lymph node effaced by a diffuse proliferation of small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with cleaved nuclei (centrocytes) and larger cells with vesicular nuclei and prominent nucleoli (centroblasts). Scattered tingible body macrophages are present. Immunohistochemical staining reveals the neoplastic cells are positive for CD19, CD20, CD10, and BCL2, but negative for CD5 and Cyclin D1. The proliferation index (Ki-67) is approximately 20%.

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Mantle cell lymphoma
(B) Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma
(C) Follicular lymphoma
(D) Marginal zone lymphoma
(E) Burkitt lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old woman, painless neck swelling (lymphadenopathy) for 3 months, no B symptoms. This presentation is typical for indolent lymphomas.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender, rubbery mass in the supraclavicular region. This consistency is often associated with lymphoma.
- **Histopathology:** Lymph node effaced by small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with cleaved nuclei (centrocytes) and larger cells (centroblasts). Presence of tingible body macrophages. This morphology is characteristic of follicular centers.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD19, CD20 (pan-B cell markers), CD10 (follicular dendritic cell marker, positive in follicular lymphoma), and BCL2 (anti-apoptotic protein, overexpressed in follicular lymphoma due to t(14;18) translocation). Negative for CD5 (typically negative in follicular lymphoma, positive in mantle cell lymphoma and chronic lymphocytic leukemia/small lymphocytic lymphoma) and Cyclin D1 (typically negative in follicular lymphoma, positive in mantle cell lymphoma).
- **Proliferation Index (Ki-67):** 20%. This indicates a relatively low proliferation rate, consistent with an indolent lymphoma like follicular lymphoma.

These clues collectively point towards a B-cell lymphoma arising from germinal center B cells, specifically follicular lymphoma. The characteristic morphology (centrocytes, centroblasts, tingible body macrophages), immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL2+), and low Ki-67 index are hallmarks of follicular lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Follicular Lymphoma:** Arises from germinal center B lymphocytes within secondary lymphoid follicles.
- **Morphology:** Characterized by a mixture of small cleaved cells (centrocytes) and large cells (centroblasts), often with prominent tingible body macrophages (macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic cells).
- **Immunophenotype:** Typically positive for pan-B cell markers (CD19, CD20), CD10 (a marker of germinal center B cells), and BCL2 (due to the characteristic t(14;18)(q32;q21) translocation, which juxtaposes the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18 with the immunoglobulin heavy chain gene locus on chromosome 14, leading to overexpression of the anti-apoptotic BCL2 protein). It is negative for CD5 and Cyclin D1.
- **Genetics:** The t(14;18) translocation is present in ~85% of cases.
- **Clinical Course:** Typically indolent, but can transform into diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL).
- **Mechanism:** The BCL2 overexpression inhibits apoptosis, allowing the accumulation of germinal center B cells and contributing to the development of the lymphoma.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Mantle cell lymphoma:** Arises from mantle zone B cells. Characterized by small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with irregular nuclei. Immunophenotype is typically CD5+, Cyclin D1+, CD10-, BCL2 variable. The t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation involving the CCND1 gene (encoding Cyclin D1) is characteristic. This option is incorrect because the vignette describes CD10+ and Cyclin D1- cells, and the morphology (centrocytes/centroblasts) is not typical for mantle cell lymphoma.
- (B) **Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL):** The most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Characterized by a diffuse proliferation of large lymphoid cells. Immunophenotype is variable but typically CD10 variable, BCL2 variable. Morphology is typically large cells with prominent nucleoli, lacking the characteristic follicular pattern and tingible body macrophages seen in follicular lymphoma. While DLBCL can arise from follicular lymphoma transformation, the described morphology and immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL2+) are more consistent with primary follicular lymphoma.
- (D) **Marginal zone lymphoma:** Arises from B cells in the marginal zone of lymphoid follicles. Includes extranodal marginal zone lymphoma (MALT lymphoma), nodal marginal zone lymphoma, and splenic marginal zone lymphoma. Morphology typically shows small lymphocytes, often with monocytoid features. Immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD79a+, CD5 variable, CD10-, BCL2 variable. This option is incorrect due to the CD10+ and BCL2+ immunophenotype and the characteristic follicular morphology (centrocytes/centroblasts).
- (E) **Burkitt lymphoma:** A highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma. Characterized by medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, multiple nucleoli, and abundant basophilic cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance due to numerous tingible body macrophages). Immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL2-, BCL6+. The t(8;14)(q24;q32) translocation involving the MYC gene is characteristic. This option is incorrect because the cells described are small to medium-sized with cleaved/vesicular nuclei, not the typical medium-sized cells with abundant cytoplasm seen in Burkitt lymphoma, and the BCL2 expression is positive, not negative.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Follicular Lymphoma (Correct) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma | Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma | Marginal Zone Lymphoma | Burkitt Lymphoma |
|----------------------|--------------------------------|-----------------------|--------------------------------|------------------------|-----------------|
| **Origin**           | Germinal Center B cells        | Mantle Zone B cells   | Germinal Center/Post-GC B cells | Marginal Zone B cells  | Germinal Center B cells |
| **Morphology**       | Centrocytes, Centroblasts, TBMs | Small-medium, irregular nuclei | Large cells, prominent nucleoli | Small lymphocytes, monocytoid | Medium, "starry sky" |
| **CD10**             | +                               | -                     | Variable                       | -                      | +               |
| **CD5**              | -                               | +                     | Variable                       | Variable               | -               |
| **Cyclin D1**        | -                               | +                     | Variable                       | -                      | -               |
| **BCL2**             | + (t(14;18))                    | Variable              | Variable                       | Variable               | -               |
| **Ki-67**            | Low (<20%)                      | Variable              | High (>30%)                    | Variable               | Very High (>70%) |
| **Characteristic Translocation** | t(14;18)                     | t(11;14)              | Variable                       | -                      | t(8;14)         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Follicular lymphoma is characterized by a specific immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL2+) and morphology (centrocytes, centroblasts, tingible body macrophages) reflecting its origin from germinal center B cells and the presence of the t(14;18) translocation. Differentiating it from other B-cell lymphomas relies on recognizing these key features, particularly the CD10 and BCL2 positivity, which distinguish it from mantle cell lymphoma (CD5+, Cyclin D1+), DLBCL (variable markers), and marginal zone lymphoma (CD10-, CD79a+).

<a id='question-134'></a>

### Question 134: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-34 (negative for CD23). The characteristic translocation is t(11;14).
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the identification of Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL) based on its characteristic immunophenotype (CD5+, CD23-, cyclin D1+) and genetic translocation t(11;14)(q13;q32), which leads to overexpression of cyclin D1.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to his primary care physician with a 6-month history of progressive fatigue, unintentional weight loss (15 lbs), and night sweats. He denies fever or recent infections. His past medical history is significant for hypertension controlled with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly prominent in the cervical and axillary regions, and moderate splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 45,000/µL) with a predominance of small to medium-sized lymphocytes, mild anemia (Hgb 10.5 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (platelets 80,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows numerous smudge cells. A lymph node biopsy is performed. Histopathology reveals effacement of the normal nodal architecture by sheets of small to medium-sized atypical lymphocytes with condensed chromatin and scant cytoplasm. Immunohistochemistry (IHC) is performed on the biopsy specimen. The tumor cells are positive for CD5, CD45, and cyclin D1, but negative for CD23 and CD10. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving chromosomes 11 and 14. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Follicular Lymphoma
(B) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL)
(C) Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL)
(D) Mantle Cell Lymphoma
(E) Burkitt Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, weight loss, night sweats (B symptoms), generalized lymphadenopathy, and splenomegaly. This suggests a lymphoproliferative disorder.
- **CBC Findings:** Leukocytosis with lymphocyte predominance, anemia, thrombocytopenia. This indicates bone marrow involvement and replacement by malignant lymphocytes.
- **Peripheral Smear:** Smudge cells are characteristic of CLL/SLL, but can also be seen in other lymphoproliferative disorders.
- **Lymph Node Biopsy:** Effacement of architecture by small to medium-sized atypical lymphocytes. This confirms a lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry (IHC):**
    - **CD5+:** Positive in MCL, CLL/SLL, and some T-cell lymphomas. Rules out Follicular Lymphoma (typically CD5-) and Burkitt Lymphoma (typically CD5-).
    - **CD23-:** Negative in MCL. Positive in CLL/SLL. This is a crucial distinguishing feature.
    - **CD10-:** Negative in MCL. Positive in Follicular Lymphoma and Burkitt Lymphoma.
    - **Cyclin D1+:** Positive in MCL due to the t(11;14) translocation.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(11;14)(q13;q32) is the hallmark translocation of Mantle Cell Lymphoma, leading to overexpression of the CCND1 gene (encoding cyclin D1).

Combining these findings: The patient has a B-cell lymphoma (lymphadenopathy, lymphocyte predominance) that is CD5+, CD23-, and cyclin D1+. The characteristic translocation t(11;14) is also present. This immunophenotype and genetic finding are pathognomonic for Mantle Cell Lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL):** A B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma arising from naive B-cells in the mantle zone of lymph node follicles.
- **Pathogenesis:** The defining genetic event is the t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation, which juxtaposes the CCND1 gene (encoding cyclin D1) on chromosome 11 next to the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) gene locus on chromosome 14. This leads to overexpression of cyclin D1.
- **Cyclin D1 Function:** Cyclin D1 forms a complex with cyclin-dependent kinase 4 (CDK4) or CDK6. This complex phosphorylates the retinoblastoma protein (Rb), causing its inactivation. Inactive Rb cannot bind and inhibit the E2F transcription factor, leading to uncontrolled cell cycle progression from G1 to S phase and promoting cell proliferation.
- **Immunophenotype:** MCL cells typically express B-cell markers (CD19, CD20, CD22, CD45), CD5 (also expressed in CLL/SLL and T-cell lymphomas), and cyclin D1. They are characteristically negative for CD10 (found in Follicular Lymphoma and Burkitt Lymphoma) and CD23 (found in CLL/SLL).
- **Clinical Features:** Often presents with widespread lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, and bone marrow involvement. B symptoms (fever, night sweats, weight loss) are common. Aggressive clinical course compared to CLL/SLL or Follicular Lymphoma.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Follicular Lymphoma:** Typically presents with generalized lymphadenopathy. Histologically, it shows follicular pattern with centrocytes and centroblasts. Immunophenotype is usually CD5-, CD10+, CD23+, BCL2+. The characteristic translocation is t(14;18)(q32;q21) involving the BCL2 gene. The patient's CD5+, CD23-, CD10-, and t(11;14) findings rule out Follicular Lymphoma.
- (B) **Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL):** The most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Presents with rapidly enlarging mass, often extranodal. Histologically, it shows diffuse proliferation of large atypical B-cells. Immunophenotype is variable but typically CD10-, CD23-, BCL2-. While it can be CD5+, it lacks the specific t(11;14) and cyclin D1 overexpression seen in MCL. The patient's specific immunophenotype and translocation point away from DLBCL.
- (C) **Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL):** Characterized by proliferation of small, mature-appearing lymphocytes. Often presents with lymphocytosis and lymphadenopathy. Immunophenotype is typically CD5+, CD23+, CD10-, cyclin D1-. The key distinguishing feature from MCL is CD23 positivity. The patient's CD23 negativity rules out CLL/SLL.
- (E) **Burkitt Lymphoma:** A highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma. Histologically, it shows a "starry sky" pattern with numerous tingible body macrophages. Immunophenotype is typically CD5-, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2-. The characteristic translocations involve the MYC gene on chromosome 8 (e.g., t(8;14), t(2;8), t(8;22)). The patient's CD5+, CD10-, and t(11;14) findings rule out Burkitt Lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature          | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL) | Follicular Lymphoma | CLL/SLL              | DLBCL                 | Burkitt Lymphoma      |
|-------------------|-----------------------------|----------------------|----------------------|-----------------------|-----------------------|
| **Age**           | Median ~60s                 | Median ~60s         | Median ~70s         | Variable, Median ~60s | Variable, often young |
| **Presentation**  | Lymphadenopathy, Splenomegaly | Lymphadenopathy     | Lymphocytosis, LAD   | Rapidly enlarging mass | Rapidly enlarging mass |
| **Histology**     | Small-medium cells, diffuse | Follicular pattern   | Small cells, diffuse | Large cells, diffuse  | "Starry sky" pattern  |
| **CD5**           | +                           | -                    | +                    | +/-                   | -                    |
| **CD10**          | -                           | +                    | -                    | -                     | +                    |
| **CD23**          | -                           | +                    | +                    | -                     | -                    |
| **Cyclin D1**     | +                           | -                    | -                    | -                     | -                    |
| **Translocation** | t(11;14)                    | t(14;18)             | Trisomy 12, del(13q)  | Variable              | t(8;14), t(2;8), t(8;22) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Mantle Cell Lymphoma is characterized by the t(11;14) translocation leading to cyclin D1 overexpression and an immunophenotype of CD5+, CD23-. Differentiating MCL from CLL/SLL (CD5+, CD23+) and Follicular Lymphoma (CD5-, CD23+, CD10+) is crucial. Remember the key features: MCL = t(11;14), Cyclin D1+, CD5+, CD23-.

<a id='question-135'></a>

### Question 135: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-35 (and the heavy chain locus.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the genetic basis of Burkitt lymphoma, specifically the characteristic chromosomal translocation involving the *MYC* oncogene and its translocation partner, typically an immunoglobulin heavy chain locus, leading to dysregulation of *MYC* expression and uncontrolled cell proliferation. (本题考察伯基特淋巴瘤的遗传学基础，特别是涉及 *MYC* 癌基因及其易位伴侣的特征性染色体易位，通常是免疫球蛋白重链基因座，导致 *MYC* 表达失调和细胞不受控制的增殖。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents with a rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, and generalized lymphadenopathy. He reports recent weight loss and fatigue. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass involving the mandible, along with palpable cervical and axillary lymph nodes. A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely dispersed chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. These cells are positive for CD20 and CD10, and negative for CD5 and CD23. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation between chromosomes 8 and 14, designated t(8;14)(q24;q32).

Which of the following molecular events is the most likely direct consequence of this translocation, leading to the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Fusion of the *BCL2* gene on chromosome 18 with the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus, resulting in overexpression of an anti-apoptotic protein.
(B) Fusion of the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8 with the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14, resulting in constitutive overexpression of the *MYC* transcription factor.
(C) Fusion of the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22 with the *ABL1* gene on chromosome 9, resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase.
(D) Fusion of the *IGH* gene on chromosome 14 with the *CCND1* gene on chromosome 11, resulting in overexpression of cyclin D1.
(E) Fusion of the *PAX5* gene on chromosome 19 with the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus, resulting in impaired B-cell differentiation.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old male from Equatorial Africa with rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, lymphadenopathy, weight loss, fatigue. This presentation is highly suggestive of a high-grade lymphoma, particularly Burkitt lymphoma, which is endemic in this region and often presents with jaw involvement.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender jaw mass (mandible), cervical and axillary lymphadenopathy. Confirms the presence of a rapidly growing tumor.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely dispersed chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance is often seen but not explicitly mentioned here). Immunophenotype: CD20+, CD10+, CD5-, CD23-. This immunophenotype is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma (a mature B-cell lymphoma).
- **Cytogenetic Analysis:** Translocation t(8;14)(q24;q32). This is the hallmark cytogenetic abnormality associated with the majority (around 80%) of Burkitt lymphoma cases.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the direct molecular consequence of the t(8;14) translocation leading to pathogenesis.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The t(8;14)(q24;q32) translocation places the *MYC* proto-oncogene (located at 8q24) under the control of the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) locus enhancer elements (located at 14q32).
- The IGH locus is highly active in B-cells, undergoing significant transcription and rearrangement.
- Placing *MYC* under the control of the IGH enhancer leads to its constitutive overexpression.
- *MYC* is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of numerous genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism.
- Overexpression of *MYC* drives uncontrolled cell proliferation and inhibits differentiation, characteristic features of Burkitt lymphoma.
- Therefore, the direct molecular consequence of t(8;14) is the constitutive overexpression of the *MYC* transcription factor due to its juxtaposition with the highly active IGH locus.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Fusion of *BCL2* (chromosome 18) with the IGH locus is characteristic of follicular lymphoma, t(14;18)(q32;q21). This leads to overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL2, promoting cell survival. This is incorrect for Burkitt lymphoma.
- (C): Fusion of *BCR* (chromosome 22) with *ABL1* (chromosome 9) is the Philadelphia chromosome, t(9;22)(q34;q11), characteristic of chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) and some cases of acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL). This creates the BCR-ABL fusion protein, a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. This is incorrect for Burkitt lymphoma.
- (D): Fusion of *IGH* (chromosome 14) with *CCND1* (chromosome 11) is characteristic of mantle cell lymphoma, t(11;14)(q13;q32). This leads to overexpression of cyclin D1, promoting cell cycle progression. This is incorrect for Burkitt lymphoma.
- (E): *PAX5* (chromosome 19) is a B-cell specific transcription factor crucial for B-cell development. While mutations or dysregulation of *PAX5* can occur in B-cell malignancies, a fusion with the IGH locus is not the characteristic event in Burkitt lymphoma. The primary driver in Burkitt lymphoma is *MYC* dysregulation. This is incorrect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | CML/ALL (t(9;22)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14)) |
|----------------------|---------------------------|------------------------------|-------------------|---------------------------------|
| **Characteristic Translocation** | t(8;14)(q24;q32)         | t(14;18)(q32;q21)            | t(9;22)(q34;q11)  | t(11;14)(q13;q32)                |
| **Genes Involved**    | *MYC*, *IGH*              | *BCL2*, *IGH*                | *BCR*, *ABL1*      | *CCND1*, *IGH*                   |
| **Molecular Consequence** | *MYC* overexpression      | BCL2 overexpression          | BCR-ABL fusion kinase | Cyclin D1 overexpression         |
| **Pathophysiology**   | Uncontrolled proliferation | Anti-apoptosis, survival     | Uncontrolled proliferation | Uncontrolled proliferation |
| **Typical Presentation** | Jaw mass, endemic Africa | Generalized lymphadenopathy | Leukocytosis, splenomegaly | Lymphadenopathy, often older age |
| **Immunophenotype**   | CD20+, CD10+, CD5-, CD23- | CD20+, CD10+, CD5-, CD23-     | CD34+, CD38+ (blasts) | CD20+, CD5+, CD23-                |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(8;14) translocation in Burkitt lymphoma juxtaposes the *MYC* oncogene with the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus, leading to constitutive overexpression of *MYC* and driving rapid cell proliferation. This is a key association to memorize. Differentiate this from other common B-cell lymphoma translocations like t(14;18) in follicular lymphoma (BCL2 overexpression) and t(11;14) in mantle cell lymphoma (Cyclin D1 overexpression). Remember the Philadelphia chromosome t(9;22) is associated with CML/ALL (BCR-ABL).

<a id='question-136'></a>

### Question 136: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 03-36 (Marginal zone lymphoma (MALToma) is a diverse group)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of the characteristic cytogenetic abnormality associated with MALT lymphoma, specifically the t(11;18) translocation leading to API2-MALT1 fusion protein, and its distinction from other B-cell lymphomas with different genetic features. (识别MALT淋巴瘤的特征性细胞遗传学异常，特别是t(11;18)易位导致API2-MALT1融合蛋白，并将其与其他具有不同遗传特征的B细胞淋巴瘤区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to his primary care physician with a 6-month history of persistent abdominal discomfort and bloating. He denies fever, night sweats, or weight loss. His past medical history is significant for chronic Helicobacter pylori infection, which was diagnosed 5 years ago but never treated. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. A complete blood count (CBC) shows mild anemia (Hgb 11.5 g/dL) and lymphocytosis (WBC 12,000/µL with 60% lymphocytes). An upper endoscopy reveals diffuse gastritis. Biopsies of the gastric mucosa show infiltration by monotonous small lymphocytes with round nuclei and pale cytoplasm, forming lymphoid follicles without prominent germinal centers. Immunohistochemistry reveals the lymphocytes are positive for CD19, CD20, CD79a, and BCL2, and negative for CD10 and BCL6. Cytogenetic analysis of the gastric biopsy reveals a translocation involving chromosomes 11 and 18.

Which of the following cytogenetic abnormalities is most likely present in this patient's gastric MALT lymphoma?

(A) t(14;18)(q32;q21) resulting in BCL2 overexpression
(B) t(8;14)(q24;q32) resulting in MYC rearrangement
(C) t(11;18)(q23;q21) resulting in API2-MALT1 fusion
(D) del(13q) resulting in loss of RB1 function
(E) t(11;22)(q23;q12) resulting in EWSR1-FLI1 fusion

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with chronic abdominal discomfort, bloating, mild splenomegaly, mild anemia, and lymphocytosis. This suggests a possible lymphoproliferative disorder, potentially involving the gastrointestinal tract.
- **Past Medical History:** Untreated chronic *Helicobacter pylori* infection. This is a crucial clue, as chronic *H. pylori* gastritis is strongly associated with the development of gastric MALT lymphoma.
- **Endoscopy & Biopsy:** Diffuse gastritis with infiltration by monotonous small lymphocytes forming lymphoid follicles without prominent germinal centers. This histological description is characteristic of MALT lymphoma (Mucosa-Associated Lymphoid Tissue lymphoma). The lack of prominent germinal centers helps distinguish it from follicular lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD19+, CD20+, CD79a+ (B-cell markers), BCL2+ (consistent with MALT lymphoma, often positive but not strongly), CD10-, BCL6-. The negativity for CD10 and BCL6 is important as it helps differentiate MALT lymphoma from follicular lymphoma (typically CD10+, BCL6+) and diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL) (often CD10+, BCL6+).
- **Cytogenetic Analysis:** A translocation involving chromosomes 11 and 18 is detected. This is the key finding pointing towards the specific genetic abnormality associated with a subset of MALT lymphomas.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific cytogenetic abnormality.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **MALT Lymphoma:** A low-grade B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma typically arising in extranodal sites, often associated with chronic inflammation (e.g., *H. pylori* gastritis, Sjögren's syndrome).
- **Pathogenesis:** Chronic antigenic stimulation (e.g., by *H. pylori*) leads to the accumulation of B-cells in the affected tissue. In a subset of cases, this chronic stimulation results in a specific chromosomal translocation.
- **t(11;18)(q23;q21):** This translocation fuses the *API2* gene on chromosome 11 with the *MALT1* gene on chromosome 18. The resulting API2-MALT1 fusion protein has constitutive nuclear localization and oncogenic activity, driving B-cell proliferation and survival. This translocation is found in approximately 40-50% of gastric MALT lymphomas associated with *H. pylori*. Eradication of *H. pylori* can lead to regression of the lymphoma in many cases, especially those with this translocation.
- **Other B-cell Lymphomas & Translocations:**
    - **Follicular Lymphoma:** t(14;18)(q32;q21) involving *IGH* and *BCL2* genes, leading to BCL2 overexpression and inhibition of apoptosis.
    - **Burkitt Lymphoma:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) involving *MYC* and *IGH* genes, leading to MYC overexpression and uncontrolled cell proliferation.
    - **Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** t(11;14)(q13;q32) involving *CCND1* (Cyclin D1) and *IGH* genes, leading to Cyclin D1 overexpression and cell cycle dysregulation.
    - **Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL):** Often associated with deletions, particularly del(13q) involving the *RB1* tumor suppressor gene, or trisomy 12.
    - **Ewing Sarcoma:** t(11;22)(q23;q12) involving *EWSR1* and *FLI1* genes, leading to an EWSR1-FLI1 fusion protein, typically found in bone and soft tissue sarcomas, not lymphomas.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): t(14;18)(q32;q21) is the hallmark translocation of Follicular Lymphoma, leading to BCL2 overexpression. This patient's histology (lack of germinal centers) and immunophenotype (CD10-, BCL6-) are not typical for follicular lymphoma.
- (B): t(8;14)(q24;q32) is characteristic of Burkitt Lymphoma, a high-grade B-cell lymphoma with a different clinical presentation (often rapidly growing mass, systemic symptoms) and morphology (starry sky pattern).
- (D): del(13q) involving the *RB1* gene is commonly found in Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL), which typically presents with peripheral lymphocytosis but usually involves mature-appearing lymphocytes and a different clinical course and morphology than described here.
- (E): t(11;22)(q23;q12) is the characteristic translocation of Ewing Sarcoma, a malignant bone and soft tissue tumor, not a lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Gastric MALT Lymphoma (t(11;18)) | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | CLL/SLL (del(13q)) | Ewing Sarcoma (t(11;22)) |
|----------------------|-----------------------------------|---------------------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------|---------------------------|
| **Associated Condition** | *H. pylori* gastritis            | None specific                  | EBV infection (endemic)     | None specific      | None specific             |
| **Histology**        | Lymphoid follicles, no germinal centers | Follicular pattern, germinal centers | Diffuse, starry sky       | Diffuse, mature lymphocytes | Small round blue cells    |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD19+, CD20+, CD79a+, BCL2+, CD10-, BCL6- | CD19+, CD20+, CD79a+, BCL2+, CD10+, BCL6+ | CD19+, CD20+, CD79a+, CD10+, BCL6+ | CD19+, CD20+, CD5+, CD23+ | CD99+, FLI1+              |
| **Cytogenetics**     | t(11;18)(q23;q21)                 | t(14;18)(q32;q21)               | t(8;14)(q24;q32)             | del(13q)           | t(11;22)(q23;q12)         |
| **Fusion Gene**      | API2-MALT1                       | BCL2                           | MYC                         | RB1 loss           | EWSR1-FLI1                |
| **Clinical Course**  | Low-grade, indolent               | Indolent                        | High-grade, aggressive      | Indolent           | Aggressive                |
| **Tissue Origin**    | Mucosa (gastric)                  | Lymph node, bone marrow         | Lymph node, bone marrow     | Blood, lymph node  | Bone, soft tissue         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Gastric MALT lymphoma associated with *H. pylori* infection frequently harbors the t(11;18)(q23;q21) translocation, creating the API2-MALT1 fusion protein. This distinguishes it from other B-cell lymphomas like follicular lymphoma (t(14;18)), Burkitt lymphoma (t(8;14)), and mantle cell lymphoma (t(11;14)). Remember the association between chronic *H. pylori* and gastric MALT lymphoma, and the specific translocation involved. Key differentials include other gastric lymphomas (e.g., DLBCL, follicular lymphoma) and non-lymphomatous gastric conditions.
